The check is an allowlist: a schema node may carry only `type`, `properties`, `required`, `additionalProperties`, `items`, `enum`, `const`, `anyOf`, `allOf`, `$ref`, `default`, `format`, `minItems`, `description` and `title`, plus `$defs` and `definitions` at the root only, and any other keyword is refused with its name in the message. The list is the structured-outputs documentation's "Supported features" plus `description` and `title`; the two annotations are there because the SDK's own `transform_schema` in anthropic 2.3.0 (`lib/_parse/_transform.py`) sends them to the API. The first version listed what to refuse and accepted every keyword it did not know, which fails open; it was changed to list what to allow before the commit. The review of that fix also found the reference walk reading property names as keywords (a property named `default`, `enum` or `const` hid its whole subtree, which let a recursive definition crash the complexity count and hid a `$ref` inside `allOf`); that was fixed before the commit too. Each refusal has its own test: a root that is not an object; an object (at any depth) without `additionalProperties` false, and `additionalProperties` anywhere that is not false; a `type` outside object, array, string, integer, number, boolean and null, or a list that repeats a type; a subschema that is not an object, a boolean subschema included; a subschema that declares no shape, meaning none of `type`, `$ref`, `anyOf` or `allOf` (so `{}`, a lone `description` and an enum or const with no type admit any value and are refused; the SDK's own `transform_schema` in anthropic 2.3.0 raises for a schema with none of `type`, `anyOf`, `oneOf` or `allOf` after its `$ref` early return, and refuses an enum with no type); a node with `properties` and a non-object type that is not closed; a non-finite number (`NaN`, `Infinity`, `-Infinity`, or a literal too large for a float), which is not valid JSON for a request body, so `canonical_json` refuses it and `ResponseFormat.from_schema` turns that into `InputValidationError`; `required` that is not a list of distinct strings each naming a property of the same object; a `description` or `title` that is not a string; an empty enum or one with a value that is not a primitive; a `const` that is not a primitive; a `format` outside date-time, time, date, duration, email, hostname, uri, ipv4, ipv6 and uuid; `minItems` other than 0 or 1; a `$ref` that is external, names a missing definition, or is recursive; a `$ref` anywhere inside an `allOf` (the documentation lists `allOf` with `$ref` as unsupported, and this is the reading that fails loud); `$defs` or `definitions` below the root; and a definition that no `$ref` reaches. References are collected structurally: one walk over the schema positions (property values, items, `anyOf` and `allOf` branches, and the root definitions where asked) serves the recursion check, the used-definition check, the `allOf` rule and the complexity count, a property name is never read as a keyword and an enum, const or default value is never walked. The definition, reference, recursion and complexity checks live in `llm/schema_graph.py`, the keyword and value checks in `llm/schema.py`. The two documented complexity limits are counted from the root with each `$ref` followed into its definition at every use: at most 24 optional properties (a property its object's `required` list does not name) and at most 16 nodes that use `anyOf` or a list-valued `type`. Because every definition must be used and reuse is counted at each use, the counts are upper bounds whether the API counts a reused definition once or at each use. The API's further internal limit ("Schema is too complex for compilation") cannot be checked locally; it is owed to the adapter commit, which maps that 400 to a typed error. The provider's schema is a looser projection of the caller's strict model, and the caller's own model validates the answer again. A message and the system text must contain a non-whitespace character, since the Messages API refuses a whitespace-only text block. Owed to the first caller, the memo commit: a projector from a strict Pydantic model to this subset, keeping only the allowed keywords.
- Usage (`Usage`) holds the uncached input, the output, the cache writes split into five-minute and one-hour parts, the cache reads and a thinking meter. `output_tokens` is the inclusive total that is billed and the thinking meter is part of it and never added (D56); `prompt_tokens`, which sets a price band, is input plus both writes plus the reads. `usage_from_counts` maps a provider's reported counts to a `Usage` and refuses with `UnpricedUsage` a cache write with no five-minute and one-hour split, half a split, a split that does not add up to the reported total, a negative count and a thinking meter above the output; the rate is never guessed.
- The fingerprint of a request is the SHA-256 of the canonical JSON of eight fields: a format version (`llm-request-v1`), the model, the system text, its cache lifetime, the messages, the schema text, the output limit and the effort. The purpose and the response format's name are labels and are left out, so renaming a call site never breaks a replay. The function is called `request_fingerprint` because a function named `fingerprint` in the package namespace shadowed its own module, which broke `import listinglens.llm.fingerprint as module`; this was found while testing and fixed before the commit.
- The error family: `core/errors/llm.py` holds `ModelError` (the family base) and `UnpricedUsage`; each later class arrives in the commit that raises it (the price table's and the budget's, the recordings', the adapter's). The leaf guard that D55 owed is paid here: every family module under `core/errors/` imports only the standard library and `core.errors.base`, `base` imports only the standard library, and `__init__` imports only the family modules, with a self-test so a weakened guard is caught.
- The key setting waits for the adapter commit, with `.env.example`, the README and the canaries. A probe on pydantic-settings 2.15.0 with a canary key found: only `LISTINGLENS_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is read, from the environment or `.env`, and `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in either is ignored; a `SecretStr` is masked in `repr`, `str`, an f-string, `model_dump()`, `model_dump(mode="json")`, `model_dump_json()`, the JSON schema, a deep copy and a logging call, and `json.dumps` of the raw dump raises without the key in its text; it is not masked in `pickle`, in `exc.errors()` of a settings error (which carries the offending `input`, while the `str` and `repr` of the error do not), or when a plain model is given a wrong-typed container that holds it. So the key gets no shape validator (a blank value becomes "not configured" through a before-validator that never raises), nothing ever prints `errors()` for it, and no model of ours holds it except the settings class.
- Not in the table, by ruling: the batch discount. Phase 4 makes no batch call and whether the discount applies to the cache lines is unverified, so the price table (next commit) carries no rule for it; it is added in Phase 5 if the evaluation uses batch, verified then.
- Evidence: the fingerprint of a hand-written canonical request, hashed with plain `hashlib` in a scratch script that imports nothing from `listinglens`, equals the code's (`0dd86a1e1b361154a33370db72172cbc0310e3c36c32e2c001cd6a53271dfc13`), and each of the eight fields changes it while the purpose and the name do not; the request validation, every schema refusal and acceptance, `usage_from_counts` and the guards, with self-tests, are in `tests/test_llm_models.py`, `test_llm_fingerprint.py`, `test_llm_schema_keywords.py`, `test_llm_schema_structure.py`, `test_llm_schema_limits.py` (with `tests/llm_schema_support.py`), `test_llm_usage.py`, `test_llm_boundaries.py` and `test_core_errors_leaf.py`. 116 scripted mutations, each restored byte for byte (hashes compared), were each caught by a named test: a final or first assistant turn accepted, turns that do not alternate, a zero output limit, a provider ceiling in the neutral request, the schema of a format unchecked, a refusal carrying text or a category on another stop, extra request fields allowed, every allowlist rule removed one by one (the keyword allowlist, the boolean and non-object subschema, the type names and repeats, required in its three parts, additionalProperties outside objects and on objects with or without a type, the annotations, enum, const, format, minItems, external, missing and recursive references, a reference inside allOf, data keys read as references, the root-only rule, unused definitions, each place a subschema is walked, each complexity counter with its off-by-one, its union and optional cases and its reference expansion and reuse counting, the shape rule and each of its four keywords, the structural reference walk (both generic variants), non-finite numbers in the text, after parsing and in a dict, the typed refusal in `from_schema`, `schema_graph.py` importing a banned module or reading the environment, and whitespace-only turns and system text), an unsplit or half-split or non-adding cache write, a negative count, thinking above output in the helper and in the model, thinking added to output, the prompt without its reads or one-hour writes, the purpose or the name in the fingerprint, each of the eight fingerprint fields and the roles dropped, `UnpricedUsage` outside the family, four weakenings of the leaf guard and four bad imports into the error package, and every banned or unapproved import, an environment read and a `print` in `llm/`. Two survived the first pass of the allowlist (an object with properties and no type, and a `$ref` key inside a `default` read as a reference); each got a test and was caught, and the second fix replaced both with the structural walk and the shape rule. Before the allowlist, the denylist version had one survivor, an external reference that the missing-definition check still refused, which was killed by asserting the specific reason.
- Options considered: one commit for the whole package (rejected: about 900 lines of source with 1,000 of tests); an async or streaming protocol (rejected: D42); a refusal as an error (rejected: the caller decides); the schema as a dict (rejected: not hashable, no exact fingerprint); an exact token count as the second method (rejected: a bound is what the budget needs and another provider may not count); a provider ceiling in the request (rejected: it is a transport rule); the key setting in this commit (rejected: nothing consumes it yet).
- Why: the reading call and the memo writer must not depend on a provider's types, and a Phase 5 bake-off should need one adapter file; the neutral request is also the narrowest one that both callers can use, so a field a model rejects cannot be sent.
- Revisit if: a second provider needs a field the request lacks (a tool, a sampling control), a Phase 5 judge needs a different stop vocabulary, or a caller needs a schema pattern.

### D56: The Anthropic SDK is pinned at 2.3.0, and the rule for choosing a release (amends D53)
- Date: 2026-11-14
- Decision: `anthropic==2.3.0` joins `[project.dependencies]` as its first entry, a core dependency and not an optional extra, and `uv.lock` gains four packages and nothing else changes. No code uses it yet: the provider-neutral interface comes next and the adapter after it. The adapter imports it lazily, only when a key is configured, because a cold import took about 5 seconds in a scratch environment (about 3.7 seconds for `mcp`, for scale) and the four XBRL tools never need it. The lock was made with the venv's uv 0.13.4 and its header is unchanged (`version = 1`, `revision = 5`, `requires-python = "==3.12.*"`). No guard, README or `.env.example` change belongs to this commit; the guard that only `llm/anthropic_client.py` imports `anthropic` arrives with the adapter, and the README and `.env.example` change with the key handling.
- The rule for choosing a release, amending D53: D53 said "the oldest release at least two weeks old whose types cover everything the adapter uses". That wording was wrong, because the oldest qualifying release can lack later bug and security fixes. The rule is the newest release published at least 14 days before the day of the plan, provided its types cover what the adapter uses and `uv audit` is clean. It is evaluated on the day of the plan and the pin is not chased afterwards. D53's own text stays as written, as the log of what was said then.
- Releases (PyPI upload times, UTC, none yanked): 1.17.0 on 2026-10-20, 2.0.0 and 2.1.0 on 10-21, 2.2.0 and 2.3.0 on 10-23 (2.3.0 at 23:18), 2.4.0 on 11-02, 2.5.0 on 11-04, 2.6.0 on 11-05, 2.7.0 on 11-09, 2.8.0 on 11-10 (the latest). The plan was written on 2026-11-14, so the cut-off was 2026-10-31 and 2.3.0 is the newest release on or before it.
- What 2.3.0 covers, read from the installed source of a scratch environment (the package ships `py.typed`): `messages.create` takes `output_config` as a `TypedDict` with `effort` (low, medium, high, xhigh, max) and `format` (a JSON schema: `type` "json_schema" and `schema`); `thinking` takes a typed adaptive configuration with `display` ("summarized" or "omitted"); `Usage` has `input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `cache_creation_input_tokens`, `cache_read_input_tokens`, the `cache_creation` split (`ephemeral_5m_input_tokens`, `ephemeral_1h_input_tokens`) and `output_tokens_details` with `thinking_tokens`; `Message.stop_reason` includes "refusal" and `stop_details` carries the refusal category; every request parameter is a `TypedDict` or a `Literal`. A scratch file shaped like the future adapter call (an explicit key and base URL, structured output, adaptive thinking, every usage field, the refusal check) passes `mypy --strict` with this repository's configuration, and a one-line probe with a deliberate `Any` is rejected by the same run, so the no-`Any` rule was active and our code needs no `Any` and no `type: ignore`. This settles a point D53 left open: the type's own documentation says `output_tokens` "remains the inclusive, authoritative total used for billing" and `thinking_tokens` is never more than `output_tokens`, so thinking is a part of the output tokens and never an addition to them; it is a meter measured by re-tokenizing the raw reasoning, not the bill.
- The lock: 55 packages instead of 51. Added: `anthropic` 2.3.0, `docstring-parser` 0.18.0, `jiter` 0.19.0 and `sniffio` 1.3.1. A parsed comparison of the old and new lock files shows nothing removed, no version moved (httpx 0.28.1, pydantic 2.13.5, pydantic-settings 2.15.0, pyyaml 6.0.3, mcp 2.3.0, httpx2 2.16.0, opentelemetry-api 1.45.2, certifi 2026.10.19 and anyio 4.15.1 among them), no dependency list changed except the project's own, and no existing wheel or sdist hash changed; the diff is 65 added lines and none removed. `anthropic`, `docstring-parser` and `sniffio` are pure wheels, and `jiter` has `cp312` wheels for `win_amd64` and `manylinux2014_x86_64`, so CI on Ubuntu compiles nothing. Licenses: `anthropic` Apache-2.0, `docstring-parser` MIT, `jiter` MIT, `sniffio` MIT OR Apache-2.0. `uv audit` reports no known vulnerabilities in 54 packages. Installed size is about 27 MB for `anthropic` and under 1 MB for the other three together. The suite is unchanged at 2,983 passed and 3 xfailed in a fresh environment synced from the lock.
- The environment variables 2.3.0 reads. The plain client reads `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, `ANTHROPIC_CUSTOM_HEADERS`, `ANTHROPIC_WEBHOOK_SIGNING_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_LOG` (at import) and four tracing switches (`ANTHROPIC_OPEN_TELEMETRY`, `ANTHROPIC_OPEN_TELEMETRY_TRACES_CONTENT_MODE`, `ANTHROPIC_OPEN_TELEMETRY_TRACES_MAX_CONTENT_BYTES`, `ANTHROPIC_OPEN_TELEMETRY_PROPAGATION`). Credential auto-discovery, which runs only when no credential argument is passed, reads `ANTHROPIC_PROFILE`, `ANTHROPIC_CONFIG_DIR`, the federation and identity-token variables, `ANTHROPIC_WORKSPACE_ID`, `ANTHROPIC_SCOPE` and `APPDATA`. The Bedrock, Vertex, Foundry, AWS and Google Cloud variables and the worker variables are not on our path. D53's finding holds on this version: with an explicit `api_key` the credential variables are not read and auto-discovery is skipped, but `base_url` falls back to `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` when it is not passed, and `ANTHROPIC_CUSTOM_HEADERS` is always merged into the default headers.
- Checks owed to the adapter commit, worded as checks to make; its plan pass decides each with evidence. Pass an explicit `api_key` and `base_url`, and check, failing closed, that the client's base URL is the fixed host and that its default headers hold nothing beyond ours. Pass `open_telemetry=False` (the SDK emits spans through `opentelemetry-api`, which is no-op without a provider, and the content-mode switch would add request content to spans if a provider ever existed). `ANTHROPIC_LOG=debug`, read at import, sets the `anthropic` and `httpx2` loggers to debug and installs a root stderr handler, and at that level the SDK logs the request options including the request JSON, which here is filing text; prove with a canary what reaches stderr, and decide against a user's explicit `ANTHROPIC_LOG` whether the adapter sets those two loggers to warning after import. Plant the variables as canaries: `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, `ANTHROPIC_CUSTOM_HEADERS`, `ANTHROPIC_PROFILE`, `ANTHROPIC_CONFIG_DIR`, `ANTHROPIC_LOG` and the four tracing switches. Add the import guard (only `llm/anthropic_client.py` imports `anthropic`) and make the import lazy.
- The proxy question is open and is decided at the adapter's plan pass, with options and evidence on four points, not here. What this commit found: the SDK's default HTTP client builds proxy mounts from the environment's proxy settings (the `HTTP_PROXY`, `HTTPS_PROXY`, `ALL_PROXY` and `NO_PROXY` variables and, on Windows, the system setting) regardless of its `trust_env` argument; in a scratch test with `HTTPS_PROXY` pointing at a dead local port the route for `api.anthropic.com` went through the proxy for the default client and for `trust_env=False`, and went direct only when `mounts` mapping `http://`, `https://` and `all://` to `None` was passed, which is typed and needs no `httpx2` import. On the certificate store: the SDK's own code and `httpx2` do not use `certifi`; `httpx2` delegates to `httpcore2`, whose `default_ssl_context` honors `SSL_CERT_FILE` and `SSL_CERT_DIR` and otherwise uses the operating system's store through `truststore` on Windows and macOS and OpenSSL's compiled-in paths on Linux, whereas the SEC transport (`httpx` 0.28.1) verifies against `certifi`; so the two clients can trust different roots. To weigh at that pass: whether a standard HTTPS proxy only tunnels, so that TLS stays end to end and the proxy sees the host name and not the key or the prompt (and that only a proxy whose certificate is trusted can read the traffic); the consistency with the SEC transport, which honors the environment's proxy settings and which `tests/test_server_stdio.py` relies on (D47 sets an unroutable proxy in the child's environment so that any accidental fetch fails fast); and what a user behind a network that requires a proxy would experience under each option. The going-in lean is to behave like the SEC transport unless that evidence shows a risk the SEC transport does not carry.
- Options considered: 2.8.0 (rejected: five days old on the day of the plan); the oldest qualifying release (rejected: the corrected rule); an optional extra `llm` (rejected: the reading tools and the memo command both need the package, an extra would need a CI change and a second not-available reason for a saving of 28 MB); a guard in this commit (rejected: it would add a test, and the adapter commit has the file to name); waiting for 2.4.0, which turns two weeks old on 2026-11-16 (rejected: the pin is not chased).
- Why: the newest release that has been public for two weeks has had time in the wild and carries the most fixes, and its types cover the whole adapter surface without `Any`.
- Revisit if: a newer release qualifies and fixes something we use, an advisory names 2.3.0, a type we need is missing, or the install size or import time of the package starts to matter to users of the four XBRL tools (then an extra).

### D55: The error types become a package, one module per family
- Date: 2026-11-10
- Decision: `core/errors.py` (295 lines, the cap D46 and D47 warned about) becomes the package `core/errors/`: `base.py` (`ListingLensError`), `ingestion.py` (`IngestionError`, `AllowlistReason` and the 13 leaves), `extraction.py`, `standards.py`, `validation.py` (`InputValidationError` and `ToolArgumentError`, which subclasses it), `margin.py` and `representation.py` (`UnrepresentableNumber`). `__init__.py` re-exports all 29 names through a sorted, explicit `__all__`, so every one of the 112 existing `from listinglens.core.errors import ...` statements (46 in `src`, 65 in `tests`, 1 in `scripts`, no other import form anywhere) is unchanged and no importer was touched. Each class is exactly what it was, in its original order inside its family; the only additions are the imports each file needs. The module for the input-validation family is called `validation`, not `inputs`, because in this codebase "inputs" means market inputs (`margin/inputs.py`, `InputKind`, `missing_inputs`).
- A pure move, proven with a scratch script outside the repository: the source text and the AST dump of each of the 29 classes are identical before (HEAD's `errors.py`) and after, in the same order within each family, and the per-class hashes and the table hash (`053036e2da8c7b8b61e944f342020d4be049b035758829bc3bd9a611c87191ea`) are equal; the contract of 29 names is identical (`__all__` equals the old class names, each is the class defined in its family module, and an AST walk over all 112 importers finds no imported name outside the 29; 28 of them are imported by someone, `MarginError` only through its subclass); the subclass relation over all 841 ordered pairs, every MRO, every `__init__` signature, every docstring and 28 instances built with sample arguments (message, `args`, attributes) are identical; and the collected test ids (2,986) and the suite (2,983 passed, 3 xfailed) are unchanged. The old module's incidental names `date`, `StrEnum` and `annotations` are not part of the contract and nothing imports them.
- `__module__`: each class's `__module__` changes (for example `listinglens.core.errors.ingestion.RateLimited` instead of `listinglens.core.errors.RateLimited`), and nothing was found to depend on it. The server's error translation and its log lines use `isinstance` and `type(exc).__name__`, which is the bare class name, as does the smoke script; an exception's `repr` shows the class name only; the import guards match module-name prefixes and everything here sits under `listinglens.core`; no test reads a module path or compares a traceback; nothing pickles an error. A traceback on stderr shows the longer path.
- Where Phase 4's errors live: in this package, one module per family (the model interface, the reader, the memo checker, the workflow), each added in the commit that needs it, under `ListingLensError`. The server and every layer keep one hierarchy and one import path under `core`, which every guard already allows, and the existing families (extraction, standards, margin) were already raised in their own layers and defined in `core`. Owed to the commit that adds the first new family: a guard test that every module under `core/errors/` imports only the standard library and `core.errors.base`, so the package stays a leaf and cannot start a cycle. It is not added here because this commit changes no test, and the test count is part of the proof.
- Options considered: a smaller `core/errors.py` with sibling modules and 112 rewritten importers (rejected: a 112-file diff for no gain, and a module and a directory sharing a name when new families came); each new family beside its own package, for example `llm/errors.py` (rejected: the server and the workflow would have to import `llm` for its errors, which the server's tools-and-core-only guard forbids, and the hierarchy would split); a module named `inputs` (rejected, see above).
- Why: the next change to the error types was due to split the file first (D46, D47), and the split should be a move a reviewer can prove rather than read.
- Revisit if: a family needs a dependency on another layer (its errors would then live with that layer and the server's translation would have to reach them another way), or a name outside the 29 has to be exported.

### D54: Why reported_financials is undetermined: a second caveat computed over the financial view (H1)
- Date: 2026-11-10
- Decision: a tier's `reported_financials` signal can no longer read undetermined without a reason. The engine gains `FinancialCaveat` (`absent_concepts`, `needs_multi_year`, `needs_year_end_balance`, `stale_concepts`, `tests_no_filed_figure`, in that order, concepts in declared order and once each) and `TierResult.financial_caveat`; the view gains `FinancialCaveatView` and `TierView.reported_financials_caveat`, placed immediately after `reported_financials`. It uses the vocabulary, order and pruning of the verdict's `Caveat`, minus `missing_inputs`, because the financial view assumes every market input is met. It is tier level only: a standard's reason is in its branch states and in `not_measured`. With no rule in force it is empty (`rules_in_force` already says why). The new module `margin/financial_view.py` holds `financial_leaf` (moved out of `rollup.py`, which imports it, so the leaf rule exists once), the financial lens and `build_financial_caveat`; `margin/findings.py` now runs its walk through a lens, and the real lens reproduces the old behavior exactly. `rollup.py`'s own second walk is unchanged, so `financial` is untouched, and a test holds the lens to every stored `financial` signal (tier and standard) in all 210 recorded cells and every hand-built set. The lens treats market-input and derived rules as met and skips their leaves, so their window reasons never add `needs_multi_year`, and counts only the standards that test a filed figure (D40). The invariant: with rules in force, the tier signal is undetermined exactly when this caveat is non-empty. It holds in all 210 cells and in every hand-built set, and the engine and the view each refuse a result that breaks it (the existing `InputValidationError`, no new error class).
- Published text changes: the shared description of `reported_financials` (on `TierView` and `StandardView`) now says what undetermined means and where the reason lives (for a tier, `reported_financials_caveat`; for a standard, its branches and `not_measured`), and that a standard whose rules all need market inputs tests no filed figure, reads undetermined and is left out of the tier's signal; the new field and its five members have their own descriptions; two sentences in the server instructions and the `check_standards` description tell a model to give the reason from `reported_financials_caveat`, not from the verdict's caveat, and one phrase of the `check_standards` description now lists the causes. Every other published text is unchanged. Each has an independent copy in the tests.
- D52's attribution holds for the tier signal: all 83 in-force cells whose signal is undetermined carry the multi-year member (Xiao-I continued Global reads `needs_multi_year` and nothing else), and 41 of them also carry an absent figure. Xiao-I's `nasdaq-5450b2-g` is undetermined at the standard level only because it tests no filed figure (all four of its rules are derived or external), and it is left out of the tier's signal, which is undetermined through the multi-year branch of `nasdaq-5450b3-g`. The 84 other undetermined cells are the four pathways with no initial rules in force, where the caveat is empty.
- Why `tests_no_filed_figure` exists: a hand-built tier whose every rule needs a market input (`tests/margin_sets.py` builds it) is in force, reads undetermined, and has no unknown filed figure to name, so a caveat built from gaps alone would be empty and the defect would return for that case. No Nasdaq ruleset reaches it and no recorded cell does; the member keeps the invariant exact and the case honest. It stands alone: a validator refuses it beside any other member.
- Not re-derived inside a model: neither caveat's content is checked against the tree by a model validator. The engine and view validators check structure (empty with no rules in force; non-empty exactly when the signal is undetermined with rules in force), as `TierResult` does today for the verdict's caveat, which it checks only for emptiness. The reason is that rule results carry no concept, no window and no needs-input fact, so concept names cannot be re-derived without the ruleset. The content is proven by hand-derived tables, a function-level re-derivation over all 210 cells and hand-built sets. Revisit if anything besides `rollup.py` ever builds a `TierResult`, or if a defect in either caveat reaches a result; the remedy then is to store the per-rule facts on the rule results, as its own commit.
- Finding, not fixed here: the verdict's caveat sets `needs_multi_year` from a derived or external rule's multi-year window as well, which the financial view assumes met. It is reachable: two real rules, `nasdaq-5315f3-gs-cashflow-aggregate` and `nasdaq-5315f3-gs-cashflow-positive-each-year` (external, initial Global Select), have multi-year windows, and a hand-built set shows the flag set by such a rule alone. In the 42 in-force `traditional_ipo` recorded cells it is never the only cause: the 33 cells that carry the flag each also have a concept rule with a multi-year window in an undecided branch (a re-derivation of the real pruning over the views reproduces the engine's flag in all 42, and the flag rests only on market rules in none). A test pins today's behavior with a pointer to this record.
- Evidence. Baseline: all 210 cells of `check_standards` (7 companies x 2 listing types x 3 tiers x 5 pathways, tier named, through the real service over the recorded stack) dumped before the change, SHA-256 `2be7352aeb71dbb87a8b1ff43dc7e85059b6e13b8c08bb61889bcf917fccde59`, 2,742,495 bytes; after the change `b2af20d2b583b25a5858ba30f80326335c2edcee786b89f80b0a502fa3b90dc5`, 2,776,963 bytes. Removing `tiers[0].reported_financials_caveat` from each after line gives the baseline line exactly in 210 of 210 cells, the verdict's caveat is byte for byte the same, and the key sits directly after `reported_financials`. 127 caveats are empty (84 with no rule in force, 41 clear, 2 breached) and 83 are not, with `needs_multi_year` 83, `absent_concepts` 41, `needs_year_end_balance` 11, `stale_concepts` 0 and `tests_no_filed_figure` 0. `stale_concepts` is reached by no recorded cell (ATA Creativity's stale net income sits in a standard that equity already decides, so its caveat is empty); it stays in the vocabulary and is proven by a hand-built bag. Oracles: `tests/financial_caveat_tables.py` holds the cells worked by hand from the raw companyfacts JSON and the ruleset YAML (Xiao-I continued Global, Trans American initial Capital, GE continued Global Select and Capital, Credit Suisse continued Global, ATA Creativity continued Capital, Luckin continued Global) and, for every other in-force cell with an undetermined signal, the table from an independent re-implementation of the financial view (27 rows covering the 83 cells); both agreed with the engine on the first run. Sizes (existing tests pass, no ceiling moved): one tier, model JSON 23,877 bytes (Credit Suisse, was 23,677) against 25,000, margin 1,123 (4.5 percent), wire 50,468 against 52,000, margin 1,532 (2.9 percent); three tiers, model JSON 77,561 (ATA Creativity, was 77,098) against 80,000, margin 2,439 (3.0 percent), wire 164,226 against 180,000. Mutations: 28 scripted mutations, each restored byte for byte, were each caught by a named test (the derivation returning empty, each member dropped and members swapped, the multi-year case dropped, `needs_multi_year` read with `>= 1`, market rules counted as unknown in the leaf and in the walk, a standard that tests no filed figure counted in the tier view and in the pruning, the engine validator removed or accepting a non-empty caveat on a clear tier, the engine quiet list dropping the caveat, `tests_no_filed_figure` beside other members, the view copying the verdict's multi-year, absent and stale members, the view validator removed, a caveat emitted for a clear or breached cell and for a tier with no rule in force, the field moved out of its place, the real caveat's pruning changed in two ways, and a copy of the leaf rule left in `rollup.py`). Two survived the first pass (market leaves counted in the walk, and the view copying the verdict's absent concepts) and were killed with two new hand-built cases, one with a market rule in the branch of a filed gap and one where the financial view holds while the real tree names a missing figure. The suite is 2,983 passed and 3 xfailed.
- Options considered: reusing `Caveat` with `missing_inputs` pinned empty (rejected: a field that can never be filled misleads a reader and costs bytes on every tier); folding the caveat into `rollup.py`'s second walk (rejected: it would change the code that produces `financial`); a reason enum beside the existing caveat (rejected: two words for one idea in one result is the misreading H1 is about); naming the standard-level reason too (rejected for now: the branch states and `not_measured` already show it); storing per-rule facts so a validator can re-derive content (deferred, see the revisit condition).
- Why: the question after the live host test was whether the result says why a signal is undetermined, and it did not: a model blamed missing market data when the cause was the multi-year branch. The reason is now computed once, in the engine, from the same tree and with the same pruning as the verdict's caveat, and shown beside the signal it explains.
- Revisit if: anything besides `rollup.py` builds a `TierResult`; a defect in either caveat reaches a result; a multi-year series arrives (`needs_multi_year` then needs a second look); or the finding above (a market rule's window in the verdict's caveat) matters to a reader.

### D53: Phase 4 architecture: a cited risk memo for one company, written from checked facts, and the reader that feeds it
- Date: 2026-11-10
- Decision: Phase 4 builds a fixed workflow that produces a schema-validated, cited risk memo for one company, with the narrative written by a model that never sees filing text. This record holds the settled design, the changes it makes to earlier decisions, the package architecture, the corrections found while planning, the commit order, and what later plan passes still decide. Each chunk gets its own plan pass and its own record in its own commit. The first step is the H1 field, and its decision is recorded in its commit, not here.
- Settled, scope (read the registration statement): a company still in registration has no XBRL financials, because the SEC does not require interactive data for an IPO registration statement and a new issuer's first XBRL comes with its first periodic report. The recorded fixtures agree: every IPO-stage S-1, S-1/A, F-1, F-1/A, 424B4, DRS and 8-A12B in the seven submissions files has isXBRL 0, and the first companyfacts fact is from a periodic report (Xiao-I 20-F filed 2023-04-28, Luckin 20-F 2021-06-30, Trans American 10-K 2024-07-02, ATA Creativity 20-F 2012-06-06). For a company in registration, `extract_financials` returns `no_xbrl_facts`, `check_standards` measures nothing, and the prospectus is the only source. The prospectus also states the proposed price range and the shares offered, the first price input that can be cited (a disclosed price is prose, cited with a `TextLocator`, per Phase 2's provenance note). AGM Group's follow-on F-1s of 2024 to 2026 are partly marked XBRL yet add no companyfacts facts; why is not yet known and a live read of their indexes will say. AGM Group's POS AM forms do add facts (414, D51), so "no companyfacts fact comes from a registration form" holds for the IPO-stage forms listed above and not for POS AM.
- Settled, focus and routes: ListingLens analyzes companies going through the listing process, from the document that fits their route. Phase 4 identifies all five routes of `Pathway`, each with cited evidence. It reads deeply for `traditional_ipo` only (the S-1 or F-1, its amendments and the final prospectus), the one route with encoded initial-listing rules. For any other route the memo says plainly what is not assessed, never a pass. De-SPAC deep reading is the first job of Phase 7 (two entities, D9's revisit case). F-1 documents are read now because most China small caps file them. Foreign-issuer standards stay in Phase 7.
- Settled, how the memo is written: code builds a facts layer, every figure, finding, flag, status and structure fact, each with a stable id and a list of citations (so the multi-year series can later add data without reshaping the memo). It follows the reading order of D42: `reported_financials` and the cited findings first, then standard and branch states, then what was not measured and why, and the verdict last with its caveat. Figures are worded as the reported financials, never as vouched for. The model writes only the narrative, the narrative may not contain a number, it points at fact ids, and the renderer inserts the values. A checker rejects a reference that does not resolve, a number in model-written text, a claim with no citation, and an inference without its labeled basis. Inference is its own claim kind, rendered visibly as inference (the answer to H2). A rejected memo goes back once with the checker's errors, and a second rejection fails loud with no memo. What counts as a number is calibrated: digits, currency and percent signs, written scales (thousand, million, billion and the like) and cardinal number words from two upward are rejected; "one" and ordinals are ordinary English ("one of", "first filed") and are not banned outright. The facts layer supplies counts as facts so the writer never has to count, and the plan pass for the checker brings a measured false-positive list from recorded writer outputs before the rule is frozen. The recommendation check is a short phrase list matched on word boundaries and tested never to fire on "holding company", "selling shareholders", "shareholders" or "threshold". The memo schema's complete field set is pinned as an exact literal, an allow-list that fails closed, beside the banned-name walk.
- Settled, filing text stays contained (D6 made concrete): the memo writer never sees filing text, not even quotes; it sees fact ids, kinds and values. Code finds a section by its heading first, and only that section goes to the reading call. The reading call has no tools and no side effects and answers a closed form (booleans, enums, short names) through structured outputs. Every answer carries a verbatim quote, and code verifies the quote sits at its stated span of the normalized document; no verified quote means no fact. For a number, the model points at the quote and code parses the number and its scale from the verified text. A fixture with a planted instruction inside a section must produce identical facts. Quotes reach only the person reading the memo and the checker, and in any tool result they are marked as data.
- Settled, agent shape: code decides the steps. A fixed workflow, called a workflow in these records, calls the tools through a real MCP stdio client against our own server (D1), so every memo also exercises the server. Each run has hard caps on attempts, tokens and dollars. The dollar cap is enforced before each call by bounding its worst case (counted input tokens plus the output limit at the output price) against the remaining budget, and the actual cost is recorded after the call.
- Settled, models, verified from Anthropic's documents on 2026-11-09: the reading call uses `claude-haiku-5-5` and the writing step uses `claude-sonnet-5-5`. Prices in USD per million tokens. Haiku 5.5, prompts up to 100,000 tokens: input 0.10, output 0.50, five-minute cache write 0.125, one-hour cache write 0.20, cache hit 0.01; above 100,000 tokens: 0.50, 2.50, 0.625, 1.00 and 0.05, and a request is priced by its whole prompt length, cache reads and writes included. Sonnet 5.5: input 2, output 10, five-minute write 2.50, one-hour write 4, cache hit 0.10. The Batch API is half price. Both models reject `temperature`, `top_p` and `top_k` with a 400 and reject assistant prefill; Sonnet 5.5 also rejects a forced `tool_choice`. Thinking is adaptive and counts toward the output limit. Haiku 5.5 can stop with `stop_reason` refusal. Structured outputs are stable through `output_config.format` with no beta header; their schema subset requires `additionalProperties` false and supports no string-length or numeric constraints, so the API schema is a looser projection of our strict model and our model validates the answer again. Every model call goes through one small provider-neutral interface with Claude as the only implementation, and nothing outside the adapter depends on Anthropic types, so Phase 5 can compare other providers' models and use a judge from another provider.
- Settled, cost: our own code computes the cost of every call and every run from the usage fields of the API response and a versioned price table in the repository that names its source page and the date it was checked. Langfuse shows the figure and is never its source.
- Settled, tracing: Langfuse Cloud's free Hobby plan (50,000 units a month, 30 days of data), optional, with blank keys giving a no-op tracer and identical behavior. The tracing client is a thin client first, and the Langfuse SDK only if the endpoint proves hard to match; the endpoint is verified against Langfuse's current documents in that commit's plan pass. Its host is an allow-list of Langfuse's cloud hosts, never whatever `LANGFUSE_HOST` says. Tracing never blocks or fails a memo: a failed send is logged and recorded in the run record, with no retry loop. No trace, log or result holds the Anthropic key or the SEC contact, proven by canaries as in D47.
- Settled, the key: ListingLens reads `LISTINGLENS_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` and passes it to the SDK explicitly. A general `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` set for other applications is never used, and a test proves it is ignored. The key is created, in a dedicated ListingLens workspace with its own spend limit, only before the first live run. The `.env.example` line `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` becomes `LISTINGLENS_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, and the README changes only where it names that line.
- Settled, where the memo lives: a console command, `listinglens-memo`, not an MCP tool. A memo tool would be circular under D1, a host model would retell the checked memo, and a long call blocks the loop under D42. The new reading tools are MCP tools that return checked facts, never raw filing text. The command is the product: it runs when a key is configured, bounded by the per-run caps, exits 5 without a key and 1 on a crash, as the server does. The live gate `LISTINGLENS_ALLOW_LIVE=1` is for the scripts that record fixtures and run the live check, never for the command and never run in CI.
- Settled, output: the schema-validated JSON is the record. Beside it goes one self-contained HTML page: a real typeface, hairline rules, at most one accent color used only on headings, no boxes, badges, pills or gray header bars, each citation linking to its SEC document, and inference labeled. No Markdown for now.
- Settled, policy: Anthropic's Usage Policy, effective 2026-11-12, treats a personalized recommendation to buy, sell, hold or allocate among specific investment products as high-risk finance and excludes research and analysis that is not that recommendation. So the memo schema has no field that can hold a recommendation, rating or price target, the memo says AI wrote the narrative, the existing scope note stays word for word, and Phase 5 adds an eval case that tries to draw out a buy or sell view.
- Settled, testing: no test calls a model or costs money. Model answers are recorded fixtures replayed by a fake client behind the interface. The checker, quote verification, number parsing and cost math are tested against hand-worked oracles. A gated live script (an opt-in environment flag, never run in CI) proves the real path, reports cost and makes the recordings.
- Changes to earlier decisions. D42 and D47: the statement that the server makes no model call no longer holds once the reading tools arrive; they call one through the injected interface, and without a key they return a typed not-available status as a result, so the four existing tools stay unchanged. The key reaches the server only through `ToolSettings`, the one sanctioned environment reader, so the guard that nothing under `server/` reads the environment stands. A reading call runs on the event-loop thread, as D42 requires, and blocks it for seconds; the synchronous client stays on that thread with no pool, the blocking is measured, and D47's revisit condition (a host stall) is the trigger for moving the service onto an owning thread. D42 said `get_filing` and full-text search wait for Phase 4's prompt-injection envelope; no `get_filing` is planned, because tools return checked facts and short verified quotes, never a document. The published sentences "No tool returns filing text" and "No filing text is returned" (the server instructions and three tool descriptions, with their independent copies in `tests/server_texts.py`) change when the first reading tool arrives, to allow short verified quotes marked as data, shown word for word in that commit and approved there. D4: a higher-layer cache of reading results is added, keyed by accession, document, section, normalization version, prompt version and model id; like the HTTP cache it is disposable, and it is never the eval store (D3). D5, D26 and D43: a fourth citation kind for filing metadata (accession, form, filing date, items and source); the citation shape for a submissions field that has no accession, such as the state of incorporation, is decided at the flags plan pass. D26's note that `TextLocator`'s fields may change at its first producer stands; the reader commit adds a structural check that `anchor_text` is exactly `char_end - char_start` characters long, beside the quote verification. D11: temperature 0 is impossible on current models (a 400), so repeatable grading comes from recorded inputs and cached judgments instead, and a judge from another provider is a Phase 5 matter. D8: the structured-metadata flags of Phase 4 are unweighted facts with citations; scoring and weights stay later. D14: the ingestion layer keeps its ban on form literals; form literals live in the routes, flags and reader layers. The rule that the only package importing `mcp` is `server/` gains the narrowest allowance, one file in the workflow package importing only the client and the stdio transport. The rule that network lives only in ingestion is amended in words: there are three sanctioned network surfaces, each one file with its own guard, the ingestion transport (allowlisted SEC and Nasdaq hosts), the Anthropic client file (Anthropic's fixed host) and the tracing client (Langfuse's fixed hosts); the SEC contact reaches only the first, the key only the second.
- Package architecture, as ruled. `llm/` (the name avoids a clash with the many Pydantic `models` modules): the provider-neutral interface, typed request, response, usage and refusal, the price table and cost math, and one adapter file, the only importer of the Anthropic SDK. `reader/`: document selection, HTML-to-text normalization with a version, section location, quote verification and number parsing, the closed answer forms and the reading service; the reading service is the only file there that is not pure, and the pure modules get the pure guard. `routes/` and `flags/`: pure functions of the filing metadata and a cutoff date, so a later evaluation can grade each company as of its prospectus; no MCP argument takes a date (D46 stands). Form 25 and 25-NSE are outcomes and future evaluation labels, shown as facts and never counted as flags. `memo/`: facts, narrative schema, checker, renderer and the record model. `workflow/`: the MCP client, the steps, the caps and the command. `tracing/`: the tracer interface, the no-op and the thin client. The adapter passes the key and the base URL explicitly and then checks, as an allow-list that fails closed, that the client's base URL is the fixed host and that no headers beyond ours are set, raising a typed configuration error otherwise.
- Corrections found while planning. The SDK, with an explicit key, skips the credential variables of the environment, but it still reads `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` and `ANTHROPIC_CUSTOM_HEADERS` (and `ANTHROPIC_LOG`) from the environment (read in the source of version 2.8.0, re-checked on the pinned version at the dependency commit); a stray `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` would have sent the key to another host. The fix is the explicit base URL and the fail-closed check above, with tests that plant `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, `ANTHROPIC_CUSTOM_HEADERS` and `ANTHROPIC_LOG` as canaries, and a check that `ANTHROPIC_LOG` cannot put the key or a prompt on stderr. "Nothing imports httpx2" is true of our source (the guard of D45 and D47); the process does load it, because importing the MCP client and its stdio transport brings in `httpx2` through the SDK (checked on 2026-11-10: `httpx2` loaded, `httpx` not), so the claim is worded at the source level. The `size` field of a submissions row is the whole submission (every document, exhibit and graphic), not the primary document, and no filing index has been read in this pass; so the figures quoted while planning (Xiao-I's 424B4 at 27,956,518 bytes, Luckin's 6,056,149, ATA's 3,226,757) are upper bounds, every EFFECT row is under 2.2 KB, and primary-document sizes come from the filing index in the live pass. Section-bounded reading stands either way. The selection traps in the recorded submissions: AGM Group has a 424B3 filed 2017-11-28 described as PROSPECTUS, one day before its 8-A12B of 2017-11-29, then a POS AM of 2018-02-13 and a 424B4 of 2018-02-20 described as PROSPECTUS SUPPLEMENT, and later follow-on F-1s of 2024 to 2026 and a 424B4 of 2025-03-04 that are not its IPO documents. Luckin's IPO 424B4 is dated 2019-05-17, and it has a second DRS (2019-11-25), F-1 (2020-01-07) and 424B4 (2020-01-10) that are a follow-on. Trans American has no 424B4 at all: an S-1, four S-1/A, an EFFECT of 2024-02-14 and an 8-A12G of 2024-02-23, and the G form registers a class outside an exchange, where an 8-A12B registers one on an exchange. Credit Suisse has no S-1 or F-1 in its recent filings: of 1,009 rows, 743 are 424B2 pricing supplements for structured notes, beside two F-3 and three F-3/A shelf registrations, two POS AM, four EFFECT notices and 30 filings on 424B3, so an EFFECT or a 424B3 alone never marks an IPO. These counts come from submissions_credit_suisse.json, filings.recent, read with plain json. A rule of "the latest 424B4" is therefore wrong for two of five recorded companies and has no answer for a third.
- Commit order, as ruled. First, this record. Second, H1 (the `reported_financials` caveat), with its own decision. Third, the split of `core/errors.py` (295 lines) before any new error type. Fourth, the dependency commit for the Anthropic SDK, an exact pin of the oldest release at least two weeks old whose types cover everything the adapter uses (`output_config.format`, the usage fields including the cache-creation split and `output_tokens_details`, adaptive thinking and the refusal stop reason), with the evidence in the commit. Fifth and sixth, the interface with the price table, cost math, fake client and key rules, then the adapter with its canary tests. Seventh to ninth, the memo: the facts layer over today's four tools, the narrative schema and checker, the renderer and the record. Tenth, tracing. Eleventh, the workflow and the command with the gated live run, ending in the first working memo and measured token costs for a one-tier and an initial three-tier company. Then the routes layer and its tool, the flags layer and its tool (each tool lands right after its layer, with its own word-for-word text approval and size ceiling), then the reader (document selection, which takes the route as input; normalization, sections and the first `TextLocator` producer; the reading call with quote verification, the injection fixture, the cache and its tool), then the prospectus price and offering terms into the initial-listing check (its own plan pass, possibly its own phase), and last F4, the multi-year series, with a recorded size decision.
- What later plan passes decide. The reader's pass decides: the selection rule (not approved here; the final prospectus can be a 424B1, 424B3 or 424B4, or none; the window comes from EFFECT and 8-A12B or 8-A12G; a withdrawn registration is never the IPO; the rule must reproduce a hand-read oracle for all seven recorded companies, with Credit Suisse as no route established and AGM Group settled from its documents in the live pass), the fixture policy and the repository-size cost of recording documents (located-section text fixtures and synthetic documents are the direction, decided with real primary-document sizes), the HTML parser (the standard library's parser with our own text builder is the lean), and the normalization rules, each tested: table-of-contents entries are skipped when locating a heading that appears twice, entity decoding, whitespace collapse and table-cell separation, with speed measured on the largest real document. The live pass (a started session with the contact in the environment, a scratch script outside the repository, each document fetched once, EFTS-derived sample companies, and one or two candidates in registration today) runs at the start of the reader's plan pass. Other passes decide: the checker's number rule false positives, tool names and size ceilings, the Langfuse endpoint, the citation shape of submissions fields, the offering-terms count of measurable initial rules, the exact published texts, and the scope note for results and memos that carry facts read from filing text (D45's fixed note speaks of SEC XBRL filings only, so it stays word for word on the four existing tools, and a note for text facts is approved with the first reading tool).
- Options considered: a memo tool on the server (rejected: circular under D1, retold by a host model, blocks the loop); letting the writer see quotes (rejected: filing text would reach the step that writes prose); a model-chosen workflow (rejected: code decides the steps, caps are hard); the Anthropic SDK's environment defaults (rejected: they read a general key and a base URL); the Langfuse SDK first (rejected for now: 16 extra packages and a second HTTP stack for an optional feature); a package named `models` (rejected: ambiguous with the Pydantic models already in the repository); banning every number word and ordinal in narrative text (rejected: "one of" and "first filed" are ordinary English); reading whole documents (rejected: containment sends one located section per call, so a document is never read whole).
- Why: the product is a measured risk signal with every claim cited, and a model-written memo is only worth reading if code decides every figure and every citation, the model's words are checked against them, and a document's text cannot steer anything. Putting the writer behind fact ids, the reader behind verified quotes and the key behind one adapter keeps each untrusted input in one place.
- Revisit if: a host or the checker shows the model overreaching in a way a description or a result field could prevent; a reading call stalls a host; the SDK stops reading the environment or gains a way to forbid it; Langfuse's endpoint cannot be matched by a thin client; the narrative number rule proves too strict or too loose on recorded writer outputs; or a recorded document shows a heading shape that deterministic location cannot find.

### D52: Phase 3 closes with the live host test
- Date: 2026-11-09
- Decision: Phase 3 closes. The live host step that D51 left open is done, it found no defect, and nothing in the code changes. D52 is the record of that test, the observations it produced (inputs to Phase 4, not defects), and what is carried forward.
- The host: Austin ran the test on 2026-11-09 in Claude Code's command-line tool on Windows. He added the server with `claude mcp add` at local scope, as a stdio server running the installed `listinglens-server.exe`, with the two required variables in its `env`, and removed it afterward. Claude Desktop was not used: its Microsoft Store (MSIX) build reads `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Packages\Claude_pzs8sxrjxfjjc\LocalCache\Roaming\Claude\claude_desktop_config.json`, not the file "Edit Config" opens (per a GitHub issue report, anthropics/claude-code#26073), and the current Settings layout no longer has the old Developer tab.
- The eight prompts and what came back, with roughly how long each answer took (first message to finished answer):
  - Xiao-I, continued listing, about 17 s. The tier was Global, from the directory (row 6), and Nasdaq's status was D. `reported_financials` was undetermined, with three breaches: equity -$100.6M against $10M, and total assets $15.9M and revenue $12.3M against $50M. Every figure cited the 20-F/A `0001213900-26-060270`, and the verdict was `cannot_determine`.
  - The directory stamp and hash, about 3 s. Claude quoted `1109202618:01` with its zone `not_stated`, and SHA-256 `e1eea981f1252f25976fed45242fdf3abf211bd8822eaaba5c3e1d5a80bb62e5`. This shows the host passes the whole result to the model.
  - ATA Creativity, continued listing, about 11 s. The tier was Capital and the status D. Equity was clear at $4,582,681, with its CNY twin shown and nothing converted. The net income rule was not measured, because its only figure is FY2021 (D49), and it was not reported as a breach. `reported_financials` was clear and the verdict `cannot_determine`.
  - Credit Suisse AG, continued listing, about 13 s. The result was `directory_funds_only` with `tiers` empty (D50). The three ETRACS notes were shown with status N, and Claude said "That is not a pass."
  - MacroGenics, `extract_financials`, about 10 s. Net income was -74,620,000 from the 10-K `0001125345-26-000017`, not the proxy statement's -74,620, and the anchor was that 10-K (D51).
  - AGM Group, extract then check, about 13 s. All seven figures cited the 20-F `0001185185-26-003323`, and the share count and public float were gaps. The tier was Capital and the status D. Equity was clear at $39,172,562, and the net income standard was clear at $577,826 against $500,000.
  - T-Mobile, continued listing, about 14 s. The result was `directory_mixed`: Global Select from TMUS, and Global from the senior notes TMUSI, TMUSL and TMUSZ. Claude read the L6 note (D50) and said the Global Select result is the one that matters. Total assets were `window_mismatch`, because only a Q3 balance was on hand.
  - The ticker QQQQQX: a readable not-found message, with no check run.
- Observations, not defects, recorded as Phase 4 inputs:
  - H1: Claude attributed an undetermined `reported_financials` to missing market data, when the cause was the multi-year branch. The result does not say why the signal is undetermined; a candidate fix is to expose the reason.
  - H2: the model reasons past the tools' output. It guessed why Nasdaq flags ATA Creativity and AGM Group as deficient, and assumed MacroGenics' tier without checking, labeling each as inference. The evaluation harness should score this.
  - H3: `evaluated_on` is the UTC date (D46), so a US evening session shows tomorrow's date. Claude reported it correctly.
  - H4: the live directory flags Xiao-I (AIXI), ATA Creativity (AACG) and AGM Group (AGMH) as status D. The tests pin the recorded statuses, not live ones.
- Carried forward, not fixed: L1, which now includes non-USD reporters such as Credit Suisse in CHF; L4 (the parallel ticker and exchange lists); L6 (the wording is honest, the code is unchanged); L7 (`list_filings` reads every overflow page); D49's anchor-lag revisit condition and D51's residual for unlabeled balances; the size margins, which are under 4 percent on the one-tier and three-tier `check_standards` results (D49, D50); `tests/tools_mapping.py` and `core/errors.py` at 295 lines; and Phase 2's F2 and F4: continued listing is always `cannot_determine` without market inputs, and the multi-year series is the top input.
- Options considered: closing Phase 3 on the offline and smoke evidence alone (rejected: D47 and D48 left what a real host does with the results as an open question); testing in Claude Desktop (not used this time, above); fixing H1 to H4 before closing (rejected: they are observations about the model and the live data, not defects of the tools, and they belong in Phase 4's design and evaluation).
- Why: the question after the smoke was whether a real host and a real model use the typed, cited results as designed. They did: the tiers, statuses, citations, the funds-only and mixed-tier notes and the readable error all reached the model intact, and it labeled its own inferences as such, with H1 the one misreading.
- Revisit if: a host truncates or reshapes a result, a real session exposes a defect in the tool descriptions, or Phase 4's evaluation shows the model overreaching in a way a description or a result field could prevent. Next: Phase 4, planned in a new chat.

### D51: A figure is never taken from a proxy statement, and is cited to the periodic report that carries it (F-live-2)
- Date: 2026-11-09
- Decision: two rules, refining D31 and contradicting none of it. (E) Eligibility now reads the form as well as the dates, and still never `fp` or `fy`: `periods.is_eligible` refuses a fact whose form is in `EXCERPT_FORMS` (`extraction/forms.py`), and because figure selection, the D35 corroboration index and the fiscal-year anchor all go through it, an excerpt-form fact appears nowhere: not as a source, not in a `RestatementDiagnostic` or a `CrossTagDisagreement`, not in the anchor. (C) `selection.cite` sits beside `latest_filed`: the value is still the latest-filed fact's (D31), but when that fact is not a periodic report and a periodic report among the same facts (one tag, one period, so a corroborated figure's evidence and its currency twins exist at the cited accession) carries the same value, the citation goes to the latest-filed such report, which has the form and the fiscal-year label a reader expects. The anchor (D49) cites a period and a filing, not a value, so it cites the latest-filed periodic fact at the newest end, and the latest-filed fact when none is periodic. `window_problem` is untouched, and no result model, wire size or ceiling changed.
- The lists, with their evidence. `PERIODIC_FORMS` (12): `10-K`, `10-K/A`, `10-KT`, `10-KT/A`, `10-Q`, `10-Q/A`, `10-QT`, `10-QT/A`, `20-F`, `20-F/A`, `40-F`, `40-F/A`; the SEC's annual, quarterly and transition reports and their amendments (10-KT and 10-QT are transition reports under Rule 13a-10 or 15d-10; EDGAR's form-type list as reproduced at sec-edgar.github.io/sec-edgar/filingtypes.html), restatements arrive as amendments (D31 pins a 10-Q/A), and the forms present in the six recorded fixtures are 10-K, 10-Q, 10-Q/A, 20-F and 20-F/A. `6-K` is a current report and is not in it; the smoke's copy of the list (which had it) is aligned and pinned equal to this one. `EXCERPT_FORMS` (12): `DEF 14A`, `PRE 14A`, `DEFR14A`, `PRER14A`, `DEFA14A`, `DEFM14A`, `PREM14A`, `DEF 14C`, `PRE 14C`, `DEFR14C`, `PRER14C` and `SC 13E3`, the proxy, information-statement and going-private family as EDGAR lists it (same page); the contested and non-management solicitation forms and the exempt-solicitation notices are left out, with no evidence, and the list fails open to today's behavior. `S-8` is not in it: it is a registration statement like the S-1 and S-3, and the sample shows no evidence its values differ (its 51 facts have no periodic counterpart at all); it goes on the list only if its sample facts, with tags and values, prove they are excerpts.
- AGM Group (CIK 1705402), the finding. Every target has two facts at its chosen period with equal values, the 20-F `0001185185-26-003323` (filed 2026-08-07, `fp` FY, `fy` 2025) and a POS AM `0001185185-26-003709` (filed 2026-08-25, no `fp`, no `fy`): equity 39,172,562, net income 561,630, pre-tax income 3,474,779, total assets 44,228,907, revenue 30,561,201, cash 325,338 and income from continuing operations 577,826. D31 chose the newer POS AM, so all seven figures cited a form with no label, the anchor cited it, and the three rules that read total assets in a fiscal-year window (`nasdaq-5405b4-g-assets-latest`, `nasdaq-5450b3-gs-assets-latest`, `nasdaq-5450b3-g-assets-latest`) were `window_mismatch` for want of an `FY` label. Its share count and public float are gaps for an unrelated reason: its companyfacts hold only the `us-gaap` taxonomy (442 concepts), no `dei` and no share-count tag. Recorded untrimmed (47,236 and 849,092 bytes) and joined to `CIKS`, `ALL_RECORDED`, the 210-cell sweep and the size tests.
- The sample: 51 companies (every 68th CIK, sorted, of the 3,426 in the SEC ticker map with a non-test, non-ETF Nasdaq match), 78.4 MB of companyfacts, plus AGM Group. 33 of 364 figures (9.1 percent) were cited to a non-periodic form (DEF 14A 14, S-1 7, F-1 4, 6-K 1, AGM Group's POS AM 7). Seven companies showed a wrong `net_income` taken from a DEF 14A (the pay-versus-performance table): rounded (Mattel 398,000,000 against its 10-K's 397,584,000; Inovio -85,000,000 against -84,945,901; Wave Life Sciences -204,400,000 against -204,378,000; CoreWeave -1,168,000,000 against -1,167,000,000; Scienture -41,512,000 against -41,512,264), a thousand times too small (MacroGenics, -74,620 in the DEF 14A `0001125345-26-000021` against -74,620,000 in the 10-K `0001125345-26-000017`, confirmed independently by the architect against the live companyfacts) or another measure (ATN International -23,522,000, its 10-K's `ProfitLoss`, against its `NetIncomeLoss` -14,906,000). 17 of the 42 anchors (40 percent) cited a non-periodic form, mostly DEF 14A; no anchor's end date was wrong. No rule reads `net_income` today, but `pretax_income` and `income_from_continuing_operations` are rule inputs, so the class was a real risk, not a display problem.
- How faithfully each non-periodic form repeats the periodic numbers (all tags, same 51 companies, facts with a periodic counterpart at the identical period): 8-K 689 facts, 0.0 percent differ; S-1 512, 1.6; POS AM 426, 1.9; F-1 143, 0.0; 6-K 17,107, 6.7; 6-K/A 246, 14.8; PRE 14A 60, 28.6; DEF 14A 399, 49.1. Registration statements and 8-Ks reproduce the audited statements; proxy statements excerpt them. That kind of document, not "periodic or not", is what separates them.
- What is kept: the AMC Robotics revision. A POS AM (2026-07-10) and an S-1 (2026-10-22) both say FY2025 net income is -24,926,364 where the 10-K (`0001493152-26-018144`, 2026-04-20) says -24,817,342. Two later registration statements agree with each other, so it reads as a real revision, and D31's later-filing rule keeps it: the value is the S-1's, it is cited to the S-1 (no periodic report carries it), and the diagnostic lists the 10-K. Preferring periodic forms outright, or labeled facts, would have selected the superseded 10-K; that is why the fix is a citation preference plus a deny-list, not a ranking.
- Simulation (the same 52 documents, before the code): 7 values corrected (exactly the DEF 14A net incomes), 25 citations changed, non-periodic cited figures 33 to 8 (Abits Group's F-1 x4, AMC's S-1 x3, Solowin's 6-K share count: no periodic report carries those values), anchors on a non-periodic form 18 to 0 with the anchor rule, no anchor end date changed, no figure lost; AGM Group's rules lost to the missing label 3 to 0.
- Result on the recorded bytes (dump at 78843de against this commit): Trans American, Luckin, GE, Xiao-I, ATA Creativity and Credit Suisse are byte-identical in every engine cell, every `check_standards` result, every `extract_financials` payload and every snapshot (none of them chose a non-periodic form). AGM Group: no figure value changed; the seven figures and the anchor moved from the POS AM to the 20-F (`accession_no`, `form_type`, and `fiscal_period` FY and `fiscal_year` 2025 now present); 18 of its 30 engine cells changed, every one by those citations plus the three rules newly measured, which are now `breached` (total assets 44,228,907 against 50,000,000 for Global Select and Global continued and 75,000,000 for Global initial: these were unmeasured, not passing); no verdict changed (`cannot_determine`, the market inputs); 24 of its 40 `check_standards` results changed, the same cells. Sizes with AGM Group: `resolve_company` 419 and 954 bytes (model JSON and wire), `list_filings` 6,046 and 12,758 (20) and 28,445 and 59,738 (100), `extract_financials` 4,759 and 10,272, `check_standards` 21,853 and 46,370 (one tier) and 72,444 and 153,340 (three); the maxima are unchanged except `list_filings` at 100 on the wire, 59,738 against 64,000 (was 59,718). No ceiling moved. Live read, 2026-11-09, through the real server: all seven figures and the anchor cite the 20-F with `FY`, no rule is lost to a label, the contact appeared 0 times; the live directory puts AGMH in Capital, which has no assets rule, so the three Global rules are shown on the recorded bytes only.
- Residual, recorded (not fixed): an unlabeled non-periodic fact that wins with a value different from the periodic report's, like AMC's, still has no fiscal-period label, so a balance read in a fiscal-year window stays `window_mismatch` ("no fiscal-period label"). It was found 0 times in 51 companies; fixing it would mean the domain carrying whether a period is a fiscal-year end by the annual forms' evidence, a larger change than the problem justifies today.
- Blocking: F-live-2 blocked Phase 3 from closing (ruling R8): wrong values were not rare (7 of 51 companies, one a thousand times too small), the anchors cited the wrong filing in 40 percent of companies, and the lost-measurement case sits in exactly the population the project targets, companies entering the market through a registration statement.
- Sampling etiquette: the sizing fetched the same 52 documents three times (about 240 MB in all); from here a sample is fetched once and reused in memory or a scratch cache outside the repository.
- Options considered: citation to the periodic report only (a), which does not fix the DEF 14A values; preferring periodic forms outright (b) and preferring labeled facts (c), both rejected because they select the superseded 10-K in AMC's case (and (c) used as an exclusion would also have dropped GE's unlabeled 8-K, the only source of its 2021 assets); no change (d), rejected by the sizing; an allow-list of forms, rejected because a company filing an unlisted registration form would have no figures at all.
- Why: a number from a proxy statement is an excerpt, and a figure should be cited to the filing a reader would open to check it; but the audited statements in a later registration statement can legitimately supersede an earlier report, so the later filing still wins on value.
- Revisit if: a proxy-family form not on the list shows differing values, S-8 facts are shown to be excerpts, an unlabeled non-periodic balance with a differing value is found (the residual), or a filer's only source is a form on the excerpt list. Next: Phase 3 closes (the manual host step remains).

### D50: Exchange-traded products never select a tier (L3), and what the directory cannot tell us (L6, L7)
- Date: 2026-11-06
- Decision: a row in Nasdaq's directory flagged `ETF=Y` never selects a tier. `tools/tier_selection.py` gains row 11, `directory_funds_only`: a continued listing with no tier named, at least one match, and every match flagged assesses no tier, and its note says so ("Every non-test row in Nasdaq's directory for this company's tickers is flagged ETF=Y, as exchange-traded funds and notes are. The Nasdaq operating-company standards ListingLens checks do not govern exchange-traded products, so no tier is assessed. Name a tier for a what-if."), with no rulebook section named because the standards are Nasdaq's, not ListingLens's, and no security name interpolated. Rows 1 to 10 keep their numbers; the order of evaluation is named (2 and 3), initial (1), no tickers (4), directory failed (9, raises), no match (5), all flagged (11), an unrecognized category among the unflagged matches (8), grouping of the unflagged matches (6 and 7). A flagged row beside an unflagged one is set aside, never dropped: it stays in `matches` with `etf: true` (a new field of `DirectoryMatchView`, described) and in `nasdaq_status`, and the note gains "Matched rows flagged ETF=Y are shown and did not select a tier." when the tier was chosen (or refused) from the rest. A named tier and an initial listing are the caller's own what-if and do not change. `how` gains the value `directory_funds_only` (the `TierHow` Literal, its description, the enum pin and the mapping table, where `NasdaqListing.etf` is no longer "omitted"). `directory_mixed`'s note gains: "The directory does not mark which row is the company's common equity, so a tier here may come only from its notes, preferred shares, warrants or units; check each selecting ticker's security name in nasdaq_status." `CHECK_STANDARDS` (approved word for word in D42 and D47, with an independent copy in `tests/server_texts.py`) gains four changes: the ETF sentence after "(name a tier for a what-if)."; the L6 sentence after it ("The directory does not mark which row is a company's common equity, so a tier may come from its notes, preferred shares, warrants or units; each matched row's security name is in nasdaq_status."); "undetermined means a figure is absent, is from an earlier fiscal year than the company's latest, or a rule needs a multi-year series"; and "A verdict of cannot_determine means something it needs is missing, and the caveat names it (market inputs, absent figures, a multi-year series, a year-end balance, or a figure for the latest fiscal year), so the company is neither passed nor failed." The last two are D49 text the architect missed in commit 4 of that fix: the old sentences left out stale figures, absent figures and year-end balances. D46's tier table gains row 11 and the order above.
- Evidence (recorded directory, stamp `1030202618:01`): 5,608 non-test rows; 1,283 flagged `ETF=Y`, all in Market Category G and Financial Status N; the flag agrees with the names in both directions (no flagged row lacks a fund-like word, and no unflagged row says ETF, ETN or Exchange-Traded). Nasdaq's definitions page documents the ETF column only for `otherlisted.txt`, not for `nasdaqlisted.txt`, so its meaning in our file rests on that evidence and the note says "flagged ETF=Y", never "is an ETF". No recorded company matched a flagged row before this change, so the dump of all of them differs only by `etf: false` on the matches of Xiao-I and ATA Creativity.
- Sizing (the SEC ticker map, 10,409 tickers for 8,004 companies, against that directory): 3,448 companies have a non-test match; 22 have only flagged rows (25 rows: iShares Ethereum Trust ETF `ETHA`, 21Shares Dogecoin ETF `TDOG`, Canary Litecoin ETF `LTCC`, VanEck Avalanche ETF `VAVX`, and Credit Suisse AG with three); no company mixes flagged and unflagged rows, so the mixed case is tested by hand; the other 1,258 flagged rows have no ticker the SEC maps to a company. A name rule was rejected: of 18 companies whose every match reads as a note, preferred share, warrant, unit or right by name, 7 would be wrong (the common units of Plains All American, Icahn Enterprises, Alliance Resource Partners, Dorchester Minerals and Martin Midstream, Marine Petroleum Trust's units, and Preferred Bank, whose name is the match) and 5 are SPAC units before separation, which hold common stock; 6 are truly non-common (Osisko Gold Group's warrant, Hovnanian's depositary shares of preferred, CHS Inc's preferred, Qwest Corp's notes, SWK Holdings' notes, Brookfield Property Partners' preferred units). The 106 + 214 + 365 + 257 + 104 + 56 name hits in the directory also include 165 American or Global depositary rows, which are common equity.
- L6, not fixed in code: 51 companies land in row 7 and for 50 of them the second tier comes only from notes, preferred shares or warrants (T-Mobile: common stock in Global Select, senior notes in Global, so both tiers are assessed; Xcel Energy, TPG, Zions and Gen Digital are the same). No structured field of the directory marks a row's security type, so a fix needs free text, which this project does not decide on. The behavior is unchanged (every tier is still assessed and labeled with the tickers that selected it, so the stock's tier is in the result) and the wording is now honest: the row 7 note and `CHECK_STANDARDS` say the directory cannot tell which row is the common equity, and each selecting ticker's security name is in `nasdaq_status`. Revisit with a structured source of security type, evaluated after Phase 3.
- L1 correction: Credit Suisse AG (CIK 1053092) is a US GAAP 20-F filer reporting in Swiss francs, not an IFRS or 40-F filer as D48 filed it. Its companyfacts carry the `us-gaap`, `dei`, `invest` and `srt` taxonomies and no `ifrs-full`; 587 of the 608 `us-gaap` concepts carry CHF and one carries USD (a 2012 to 2014 mortgage concept that none of the targets uses). Equity, total assets, net income, pre-tax income, revenue and cash have no USD facts, so `extract_financials` reads one figure (the cover-page share count, 4,399,680,200) and eight gaps (seven `unit_unavailable`, `public_float` `concept_not_found`), its fiscal-year anchor is null, and its last annual report covers FY2023 (20-F 0001370368-24-000013). Reading CHF is the L1 work; nothing changes here.
- L7, not fixed: `list_filings` reads every overflow page of the submissions before it filters or limits, on the first call of any size. For Credit Suisse that is 22 pages listing 42,808 filings, about 7.4 MB.
- Fixtures: Credit Suisse's submissions and companyfacts are recorded untrimmed (176,463 and 946,743 bytes) and its 22 overflow pages trimmed to 40 rows each (156,178 bytes), so `total_matching` reads 1,889 in tests, not the real count. Credit Suisse joins `CIKS`, `ALL_RECORDED`, the sweeps (180 cells) and the size tests, because it is the only recorded issuer on row 11 and the only one-figure issuer.
- Sizes after this change (UTF-8 bytes, model JSON and the compact wire form), Credit Suisse: `resolve_company` 443 and 1,010, `list_filings` 6,034 and 12,734 (20) and 28,123 and 59,066 (100), `extract_financials` 2,632 and 5,558, `check_standards` 23,677 and 50,050 for one tier (named) and 74,612 and 157,208 for three; the continued call with no tier, which assesses none, is 7,764 on the wire live.
