Nothing is hand-built, no `src/` file changed, and no network is touched: each test asserts the only URL requested was the fixture's own, and the external bag is empty. `as_of` is a fixed 2026-10-31 (a clock-dependent test is a flaky one) and continued sets use the pathway `traditional_ipo`; a test pins that the continued result is identical for all five pathways. The service takes no `as_of`, only a `ResolvedCompany` (CIK, name, `CompanyRef`).
- The matrix: Trans American, Luckin, and GE against the three continued tiers, as hand-written literals in `tests/real_matrix_tables.py`. All nine cells are `cannot_determine`. Trans American's tier-level financial signal is `undetermined` in each (2 measured rules, both breached; 7 not evaluable in Capital, 15 in Global and Global Select); Luckin's is `clear` (2 measured in Capital, 3 in Global and Global Select, all clear; 7 and 14 not evaluable); GE has nothing measured (0 of 9 in Capital, 0 of 17 in Global and Global Select) and its signal is `undetermined`. Per-standard values and each caveat (the missing inputs, absent concepts, the multi-year and year-end flags) are pinned too.
- Cited breaches (Trans American, the only breached rules in the matrix): equity -3,758,603 (`StockholdersEquity`, 10-Q Q3, 2025-09-30, accession 0001683168-26-000292) against 2,500,000 in Capital and 10,000,000 in Global and Global Select; net income -2,808,894 (`NetIncomeLoss` standing in for income from continuing operations through D35, with its identity pretax minus tax and the `parent_tag` witness, 10-K FY2024, accession 0001683168-25-005173) against 500,000 in Capital; revenue 331,645 (`Revenues`, same 10-K) against 50,000,000 in Global and Global Select.
- The tenth case: of the 54 non-empty (company, set) cells, exactly one is `does_not_meet`: Trans American, initial listing, Capital Market. Every Rule 5505 standard has an equity floor (5M, 4M, 4M) and equity is -3,758,603, so all three standards are false and the OR across them is false; the tier also has a fourth breach, net income from continuing operations -2,808,894 against 750,000. The verdict is pinned to those four measured breaches and their accessions, and its caveat is empty. All 53 others are `cannot_determine`, and none raises.
- Luckin end to end: every one of its seven figures is a USD convenience translation of CNY, every measured result carries one `TranslationNote` (rate between 7.0062 and 7.0064), and the tier's `translated_figures` carries each figure once: Capital has `StockholdersEquity` and `ProfitLoss`, Global and Global Select have `Assets`, `RevenueFromContractWithCustomerExcludingAssessedTax`, and `StockholdersEquity`. Pinned in full for equity: USD 2,230,092,000 from the 2025 20-F (accession 0001104659-26-035712), CNY twin 15,624,699,000, implied rate exactly 15,624,699,000 over 2,230,092,000, and the measured rule's citation and notes equal the tier's. In the initial Capital set equity is measured by three rules and still reported once. Trans American and GE carry no translation.
- Findings, named and not fixed (F1 to F5):
  - F1. GE's extracted total assets is a 10-Q balance labelled `Q2`, not unlabelled. The label-absent balance (205,378,000,000, form 8-K, accession 0000040545-23-000137) exists only in older 8-K facts, and the service extracts the latest period and exposes no as-of, so the no-label case cannot be reached through `extract_snapshot`. The year-end mismatch is exercised on real data with the label `Q2` (never a measured pass although 127.7 billion would clear the floor), and the no-label case is shown on the real extractor's figure (`figure_for` with `not_after`) placed alone in a hand-assembled snapshot, the one such snapshot, which resolves to `window_mismatch` with "no fiscal-period label". A `not_after` parameter on `extract_snapshot` would be new engine code and is not added.
  - F2. From EDGAR facts alone no continued-listing cell can be `does_not_meet`. No tier-common continued rule is EDGAR-measurable, and each tier has a standard whose conditions are all non-EDGAR, so an OR across the standards cannot be false without an external input. The engine can warn from filings but never convict, so chunk 15 must lead with the financial signal and the per-standard values, because the headline verdict reads `cannot_determine` until price, holders, and market makers exist.
  - F3. The one real `does_not_meet` is the initial Capital case above; it comes from the existing initial ruleset (D37) and shows a verdict resting on cited breaches.
  - F4. Multi-year branches keep standards unknown even when the latest year fails: Trans American's Capital Net Income Standard has its latest-year branch breached and its two-of-three branch unmeasurable, so the standard and the tier financial signal read `undetermined` where a reader would say failing. Correct under the logic (the second branch could still hold), and it shows a multi-year series is the most valuable next input.
  - F5. No defect in D39 or D40 was found: no window or metric that real data reaches lacked a hand-built test, and all 54 cells ran without raising. Trans American's latest equity and total assets are Q3 10-Q balances: equity is measured (an instant window takes any label) while total assets under the latest-fiscal-year window (1,106,686 against 50,000,000) is `not_evaluable`, which is the D39 year-end design working on real data.
- Mutations: 16 scripted mutations of the engine and extraction, each required to fail a real-data test. 15 were caught, including accepting any fiscal label as a year-end balance (Trans American's total assets rule becomes a measured breach and GE's 127.7 billion a measured pass; four tests fail), accepting a missing label, a changed year-end label, treating a two-of-three window as one period, dropping translation notes or the figure citation, an unduplicated translated figure, the shipped cushion, the continuing-operations fallback, the financial-view EDGAR test, `or_any` on `(FALSE, UNKNOWN)`, an unknown tier mapped to `does_not_meet`, a not-evaluable result treated as a breach, a pretended series, and the equity tag order. One survived, "caveat counts decided standards", an equivalent mutant on the recorded data: the only time a standard already holds (Luckin, Capital) every input its siblings would add is already named by the tier-common rules, so the caveat is identical either way; only a hand-built structure can tell, and D40's tests do (caught there). Files restored byte for byte.
- Why: the engine's unit tests used hand-built structures, so this is the check that real extracted figures, real citations, real conversions, and the real ruleset's structure produce honest behavior end to end, and it records what the engine can and cannot say from filings alone.
- Revisit if: an as-of is added to extraction (the label-absent case becomes reachable through the service), a price source or series is added (the matrix cells change and the tables are re-worked), or a company with a real tier-common EDGAR rule breach is recorded.

### D40: The margin rollup and tier verdict: three-valued logic, a thin flag, and a verdict that never rests on what was not measured
- Date: 2026-10-21
- Decision: `margin/rollup.py` rolls commit 1's per-rule results (D39) up the ruleset's own structure to a tier verdict. `rollup_set(standard_set, inputs, policy)` walks a `StandardSet` from `Ruleset.set_for`, calls `measure_rule` once per rule, and re-derives no per-rule state; `assess(ruleset, ...)` is the selection followed by the rollup and takes a loaded `Ruleset`, so the module still loads, fetches, and extracts nothing (the D39 import guard covers the new files).
- Logic: Kleene's three values. `clear` and `at_risk` are TRUE, `breached` is FALSE, `not_evaluable` is UNKNOWN. A branch is an AND of its rules, a requirement an OR of its branches, a standard an AND of its requirements, and a tier is the AND of every tier-common requirement and an OR across the standards. The two primitives (`and_all`, `or_any`) are written once. OR is FALSE only when every input is FALSE, so `or_any(FALSE, UNKNOWN)` is UNKNOWN. UNKNOWN is never turned into FALSE: FALSE can only come from a FALSE leaf, which is a measured breach. That is proved, not argued: `tests/test_margin_kleene.py` enumerates every assignment of {true, false, unknown} to the leaves of four structures shaped like the real ones (up to 3^8) and checks the engine against an independent possible-worlds brute force (complete each unknown both ways with plain booleans; true if every completion is true, false if every completion is false), and asserts that no assignment of only true and unknown leaves ever yields FALSE and that every FALSE value has a FALSE leaf.
- Two degenerate shapes are handled explicitly: a set with no standards (Rule 5315, tier Global Select initial, which has 6 common requirements and none) is just the AND of its common requirements, because an OR over nothing would be FALSE and make the tier unmeetable; and an empty set (no rule in force for that tier, listing type, and pathway) is `cannot_determine` with `rules_in_force` false and value UNKNOWN, because an AND over nothing is TRUE and would read as a pass. 18 of the 30 selections are non-empty; the other 12 (initial listing for the four other pathways) are the empty case.
- at_risk is a flag, not a fourth value: `thin` marks a node that is TRUE but rests on at-risk evidence, and it is false on every node that is not TRUE. An AND is thin when it holds and any part is thin; an OR is thin when it holds and every part that is true is thin, so a requirement met comfortably one way is not called thin because another way is at risk. A standard can hold thinly.
- The verdict: `does_not_meet` only when the tier value is FALSE; `at_risk` when TRUE and thin; `meets` when TRUE and not thin; `cannot_determine` when UNKNOWN (the common case: financials clear, but market makers, price, holders, or a multi-year series are unmeasured). `meets` cannot occur on the real ruleset yet: nearly every standard has a derived market-value condition, and D39 makes the engine raise `MarginConfigError` when all of a derived rule's drivers are on hand (no formula), which the rollup lets propagate. `meets`, `at_risk`, and `does_not_meet` are tested on hand-built sets and on real structures with external inputs supplied for holders, market makers, and shares.
- Result models: `BranchResult`, `RequirementResult`, `StandardResult`, and `TierResult` are frozen and strict, carry their truth and thin flag beside their children, and carry the per-rule results beneath them. Each validator re-derives value and thin from its children with the same primitives, and the tier also re-derives its verdict and its translated figures and requires the caveat to be non-empty exactly when the verdict is `cannot_determine`, so a forged result is rejected (D34, D35, D39).
- The financial signal is kept apart from the completeness gap. It is the same rollup run a second time with every rule that needs a non-EDGAR input assumed met ("if market makers, float, holders and price are all fine, how do the EDGAR-measurable conditions look?"), read as `clear`, `at_risk`, `breached`, or `undetermined`. A standard that tests nothing in EDGAR (a pure market-value standard) is `undetermined`, not vacuously clear. At the standard level a `breached` signal always means the standard's real value is FALSE (assuming more conditions met can only raise a value), and a validator enforces it. The tier-level signal summarizes the standards that test something in EDGAR (a vacuous standard would otherwise count as met and hide the rest), so it can read `breached` while the verdict is `cannot_determine`: every financial standard fails and only a market-value-only path remains open. It is not tied to the tier value. This was found by running the real sets: the first version read "clear" on an empty snapshot.
- The caveat says exactly what confirming a tier would need, counting only the conditions that still decide it: a subtree that is already TRUE or FALSE contributes nothing, and a standard counts only if the any-standard part is itself unknown. It has `missing_inputs` (deduplicated, in `InputKind` declaration order), `absent_concepts` (EDGAR figures not extracted), `needs_multi_year` (a window waits on a series), and `needs_year_end_balance` (only a quarter-end balance is on hand). The fourth part goes beyond the three planned: without it a rule blocked only by a quarter-end balance would leave an UNKNOWN tier with an empty caveat. It is empty unless the verdict is `cannot_determine`.
- Translations: every measured result under the tier contributes its figure's `TranslationNote`s, deduplicated by the figure's citation into `TranslatedFigure(figure_citation, translations)`, so a verdict resting on a convenience-translated USD figure (Luckin) still carries the rate and the home-currency pointer (D34, D39).
- Soundness note: Kleene treats each unknown leaf as independent. Two unknowns that share one missing input (the market price) can leave the tier UNKNOWN where that one input would decide it. That errs toward `cannot_determine`, never toward a false `does_not_meet`.
- Tests and mutations: hand-worked oracles independent of the engine. `tests/test_margin_kleene.py` (truth tables cell by cell, the thin rules, and the possible-worlds oracle), `test_margin_verdicts.py`, `test_margin_rollup_models.py` (forgeries), `test_margin_rollup.py` and `test_margin_findings.py` with `tests/margin_scenarios.py` (20 hand-worked verdict scenarios over synthetic structures; caveat pruning, ordering, and deduplication; translations; the financial signal), and `test_margin_tier.py` (the real ruleset structure: all 18 non-empty sets against an empty snapshot, a snapshot of only gaps, and a nine-figure snapshot are `cannot_determine` and never `does_not_meet`; a tier-common breach, a short holder count, every 5450 standard breached through the shares count, and the no-standards tier are `does_not_meet`; a derived rule with all drivers on hand stops the rollup). 53 scripted mutations (including `or_any` returning FALSE or TRUE on `(FALSE, UNKNOWN)`, an UNKNOWN tier mapped to `does_not_meet` or `meets`, not-evaluable treated as false or true, the empty-set guard, the no-standards shape, each thin rule, every validator, the caveat pruning at each level, the financial assumption, and the translation dedupe) were each caught, with the files restored byte for byte. Three survived at first (a forged tier value that agrees with a forged verdict, an unknown rule inside an already-failed branch, and a two-year window) and tests now pin each.
- Options considered: a literal "any at-risk condition anywhere makes it thin" (rejected for OR nodes: it would flag a requirement met comfortably); a fourth truth value for at risk (rejected: it is a flag on a true condition); treating no standards as FALSE (rejected: Rule 5315 would be unmeetable); catching the derived-rule error in the rollup (rejected: it hides a missing formula); a tier-level financial signal over every standard (rejected: a standard with no financial test would read as vacuously clear); a caveat over every unknown condition (rejected: it would name inputs that cannot change the answer).
- Why: the verdict is only useful if "does not meet" is a claim about the company and never about our coverage, so a missing figure, an unmeasurable condition, and an open alternative all stay UNKNOWN until a real measurement says otherwise, and the caveat names what would settle it.
- Revisit if: a price source and a derived-quantity formula are added (then `meets` becomes reachable and the rollup stays unchanged), a series is added (the multi-year branches become measurable), a standard needs a different thin rule, or the financial view should treat a non-EDGAR condition as other than met.

### D39: The margin-check engine, per-rule layer: four states, a cushion, and not-evaluable that names what is missing
- Date: 2026-10-21
- Decision: `margin/` is a pure function of a `FinancialSnapshot` (through an `Inputs` bag) and a ruleset's policy: `measure_rule(rule, inputs, policy)` returns one result per rule. No network, no extraction, no ruleset loading, no prose. This is the per-rule layer only; the rollup and the tier verdict are the next commit and the real-data tests the one after. Four states: `clear` (the figure clears the threshold by more than the cushion), `at_risk` (it meets the threshold but sits within the cushion), `breached` (it fails the threshold), and `not_evaluable` (it cannot be measured). **A missing figure is never a breach.** The two are different shapes: a measured result (clear, at risk, breached) carries the figure's citation, the rule's citation, the measured value, the threshold, the signed headroom, and the headroom ratio; a not-evaluable result carries the rule's citation and the reasons, and has no number, no headroom, and no figure citation, so it cannot be read as a breach. A model validator re-derives a measured result's state from its own numbers, so a forged state is rejected, as in D34 and D35.
- Order of work is the safety property: every reason a rule cannot be measured is collected first, and only a rule with none reaches the threshold comparison. A test sweeps all 105 real rules against an empty snapshot and against a snapshot of nothing but gaps and requires every result to be not evaluable; another changes every figure to a huge negative number and requires the set of unmeasurable rules not to change.
- Not-evaluable reasons are resolved generically by checking what a rule needs against what is on hand, not hard-coded per metric. Three kinds, kept distinct: `concept_absent` (the concept is not in the snapshot; chunk 12's `GapReason` is carried when a `ConceptGap` exists), `input_required` (the rule's `requires` names external inputs that are not on hand, and the specific `InputKind`s are named in `missing_inputs`, in `InputKind` declaration order), and `window_mismatch` (what is on hand does not fit the window). A rule can fail for more than one reason, and all are kept in the fixed order concept_absent, input_required, window_mismatch, with the first as `primary` (a cash-flow rule over three years is both `input_required` and `window_mismatch`).
- Window matching: a window needs 1 annual period (instant, latest fiscal year), m (`n_of_last_m_fiscal_years`), or `years` (`aggregate_over_fiscal_years`). A snapshot holds the latest period, so a multi-year window is a `window_mismatch` that says how many periods it needs and how many are on hand. An instant window takes a balance with any label and refuses a flow. A latest-fiscal-year window takes a full-year flow (extraction keeps only those, D31) and takes a balance only if it is the fiscal-year-end one, which is decided by the citation's fiscal-period label being `FY`; a `Q1` to `Q3` label, or no label (GE's 8-K facts), is a mismatch. The recorded fixtures label every 10-K and 20-F fact `FY` and every 10-Q fact `Q1` to `Q3`, so the test separates a year-end balance from a quarter-end one; commit 3 checks it against them. Unknown is not-evaluable, never a pass and never a breach. The concept's instant-or-flow kind is read from `extraction.aliases` rather than restated.
- Band math (exact, never rounded across an edge): headroom is `measured - threshold` for gte and gt and `threshold - measured` for lte and lt, so positive means the passing side; gt and lt fail at equality, gte and lte pass. breached if it fails; at_risk if it passes with headroom at most `cushion * |threshold|` (the edge itself is at risk); clear if it passes by strictly more. Classification multiplies and compares in a 100-digit context and never divides; the headroom ratio (`headroom / |threshold|`, `None` for a zero threshold) is reported only. The absolute threshold uses `copy_abs`, because `abs()` rounds to 28 digits. A zero threshold has a zero band: gte at 0 is at risk, anything above is clear. Pinned by a hand-written table of 37 cases and by 31-digit cases.
- The cushion: `RulesetPolicy.cushion = CushionSetting(default_ratio, basis)`, shipped as `0.10` in `policy.yaml` with its basis, an exact `Decimal` with `0 <= ratio < 1`. It is one ratio for every rule; the name leaves room for per-metric overrides, which are not built. The engine reads it from the policy it is given and never fixes it. The two synthetic policy fixtures under `tests/` gained the same block.
- The inputs seam: `Inputs` holds the snapshot's figures and gaps, an `external` mapping of `InputKind` to `Decimal` (empty today), and the annual periods on hand per concept (1 per figure). Resolution asks it four questions (`figure`, `gap`, `value_of`, `annual_periods`), so a later price adapter or series fills it without changing the resolution. There is no series field and no price source here, because nothing evaluates a series yet: a multi-year window with enough periods on hand raises a typed `MarginConfigError` rather than measuring only the latest figure. An external input that is on hand is measured directly as the value (with no figure citation, and the input named instead). A snapshot that lists a concept twice, or as both a figure and a gap, is refused.
- Loud refusals (`MarginConfigError`, a new typed error): a derived quantity whose drivers are all on hand (no formula is registered, and no market-value definition is invented); a rule or policy requiring the reported currency (`TranslationPolicy.REQUIRE_REPORTED_CURRENCY`); and a rule with a proxy under a policy that turns proxies on. None is used by the shipped ruleset. Silently measuring in USD, or ignoring a proxy, would answer a different question than the rule asks.
- Convenience translation (D34): a translated figure is still measured in USD, unconverted, and the result carries one `TranslationNote` per twin (its unit, which points at the `ReportedTwin` on the figure, the reported value, and the implied rate). `rule_verified` is carried on every result so an unverified rule, including a stayed one, is resolved and marked (D32).
- Guard: `tests/test_repo_constraints.py` forbids `margin/` from importing `httpx`, `listinglens.ingestion`, or the executable extraction modules (`service`, `figures`, `selection`, `corroboration`), the way `extraction/` is guarded; the static `extraction.aliases` table is the one allowed import. A self-test pins the finder on each.
- Tests and mutations: hand-written oracles independent of the engine, in `tests/test_margin_band.py`, `_windows.py`, `_models.py`, `_resolve.py`, `_ruleset.py`, `_inputs.py`, and `_periods.py`, with `tests/margin_factory.py` to build figures and snapshots. With a full fiscal-year snapshot exactly 22 of the 105 real rules are measured and 83 are not (12 multi-year concept rules, 25 derived, 46 external). 67 scripted mutations (including a missing figure measured as zero, a missing figure falling through to the comparison, an absence reason dropped, a missing external input measured as zero, every comparison and boundary, the exact context, `abs`, each window count and the `FY` test, the cushion read, the refusals, the validators, the inputs checks, the policy validator, the shipped value, and the guard) were each caught, with the files restored byte for byte. Two mutations survived at first (the period count reported for an external or derived rule); a test now pins that wording.
- Options considered: a single reason per not-evaluable result (rejected: a cash-flow rule fails two ways and both are worth naming); inferring the fiscal-year-end balance from the filing form (deferred: the label is already on the citation); a series field now (rejected: untestable until something evaluates a series); letting a derived rule with every driver on hand stay `input_required` (rejected: it would be untrue); a ratio test for the band (rejected: dividing can round across an edge).
- Why: the margin is only worth reporting if "we could not measure it" and "it fails" are never confused, so the engine is built around making a missing figure unable to reach a comparison, and around saying exactly what is missing so the next step knows what to supply.
- Revisit if: a price source or a series is added (extend the bag and the measurement, not the resolution), a rule needs a per-metric cushion, the fiscal-year-end label proves unreliable for a filer type (use the form), or the reported-currency or proxy policies are turned on.

### D38: The Nasdaq initial-listing ruleset, second half: Rule 5315 (Global Select), IPO circumstance
- Date: 2026-10-20
- Decision: four rule files under `standards/rulesets/nasdaq/` encode 23 records for Global Select initial listing (tier `global_select`, `listing_type: initial`, `pathways: [traditional_ipo]`), from content the architect supplied and verified against the Nasdaq rulebook (every `quoted_text` is the architect's verbatim wording). With D37 the initial-listing ruleset is complete for the IPO circumstance: 59 initial records across the three tiers, 46 continued. The other four pathways still return an empty set (`is_empty`, not a pass), the Phase 7 gap.
- Mapping: Rule 5315 is "meet 5315(e), plus one of 5315(f)(1), one of 5315(f)(2), and one of 5315(f)(3)". The schema's standard is "any one of several", so every 5315 record is tier-common (no `standard_id`, `standards` is empty) and each "meet one" group is one requirement with one branch per alternative: `nasdaq-5315f1-gs-ownership` (3 branches), `nasdaq-5315f2-gs-market-value` (3), `nasdaq-5315f3-gs-valuation` (4). A requirement holds if any branch holds and a branch holds if all its rules hold. No schema change was needed.
- 5315(e): the $4 bid price (the qualifier "if the Company is not listed on the NGM" is satisfied by a company listing in its IPO), 1,250,000 Unrestricted Publicly Held Shares, and one market-maker record at threshold 4 carrying the full conditional text. The count is 3 for a company that meets the NGM Income Standard (Rule 5405(b)(1)) or the NGM Equity Standard (Rule 5405(b)(2)); that coupling is not modeled and is not placed inside the (f)(3) branches, because those are Earnings, Capitalization, and Assets, not the 5405 standards the rule names. The record is not evaluable.
- 5315(f)(1) ownership: (a) 550 Total Holders and an average monthly trading volume of 1,100,000 shares (two rules in one branch), (b) 2,200 Total Holders, (c) one `ROUND_LOT_HOLDERS >= 450` rule with the clause that at least 50% of them each hold Unrestricted Securities worth $2,500 left in the verbatim quoted text and flagged for chunk 14, as in D37. All external, not evaluable.
- 5315(f)(2) market value (of unrestricted publicly held shares): (A) $110 million, (B) $100 million with stockholders' equity of $110 million (two rules in one branch: the market value, and `STOCKHOLDERS_EQUITY >= 110,000,000` as an instant EDGAR concept, so the equity half is checkable but the branch is not evaluable without the market value), (C) $45 million for an IPO (or an affiliate or spin-off, which shares the branch). Left out: the $70 million closed-end-fund value, a separately governed company type outside the operating-company IPO scope. These are the 5315(f)(2) values, effective 2009-04-13, SR-NASDAQ-2009-018, not the 2025-amended 5405 and 5505 values, so Rule 5315 has no pre-2025 gap (a test pins that the set is the same before and after 2025).
- 5315(f)(3) valuation: (A) Earnings is three PRETAX_INCOME rules in one branch, the first real use of the multi-year windows: `aggregate_over_fiscal_years` years 3 `gte` 11,000,000; `n_of_last_m_fiscal_years` 3 of 3 `gt` 0 (positive in each of three years, each year tested on its own, so the comparison is strict); `n_of_last_m_fiscal_years` 2 of 2 `gte` 2,200,000. All three are not evaluable until the multi-year series exists, and the table pins them as `agg3`, `3of3`, `2of2`. (B) Capitalization with Cash Flow: cash flows aggregate $27.5 million over three years and positive in each (the same two windows over an external series), average market capitalization $550 million, and `REVENUE` at least $110 million for the previous fiscal year. (C) Capitalization with Revenue: average market capitalization $850 million and `REVENUE` at least $90 million for the previous fiscal year. (D) Assets with Equity: market capitalization $160 million, `TOTAL_ASSETS` $80 million, and `STOCKHOLDERS_EQUITY` $55 million. Rule 5310(j) computes (D)'s assets and equity from the most recent financial statements, adjusted for the offering; they are encoded as instant figures like the equity records elsewhere and the offering adjustment is a chunk 14 note.
- New inputs: `MARKET_CAPITALIZATION` (usd, point in time), `AVERAGE_MARKET_CAPITALIZATION` (usd, over the prior 12 months), `CASH_FLOW` (usd, a yearly series), `AVERAGE_MONTHLY_TRADING_VOLUME` (shares, over the prior 12 months). Existing kinds are reused: `TOTAL_HOLDERS`, `ROUND_LOT_HOLDERS`, `UNRESTRICTED_PUBLICLY_HELD_SHARES`, and the derived unrestricted market value. Cash flow is an external series input and not an extraction concept, because its Nasdaq definition has not been cited and none is invented here; it can become a concept when it is. The averages are carried in the input names, a YAML comment, and this entry: no window expresses them, and none is invented.
- Chunk 14 notes: (1) an external input used with a multi-year window (cash flow) needs a series, not a single value; (2) the 12-month averages are supplied by the caller; (3) the 50% round-lot clause and the market-maker coupling are not evaluated; (4) the (f)(3)(D) offering adjustment; (5) a fiscal-year-window instant concept is evaluated only on a fiscal-year-end figure (D36).
- Citations (used exactly as supplied): `rule_url` is the 5300-series page; every record is `effective_from` 2009-04-13, SR-NASDAQ-2009-018 (https://www.sec.gov/rule-release/34-59663), `verified` true, `retrieved_on` and `verified_on` 2026-10-20, `verified_by` Austin Smith.
- Tests and mutations: `tests/test_standards_nasdaq_initial_5315.py` with the hand-written table in `tests/nasdaq_initial_5315_table.py` covers the full 23-row table (metric, window, comparison, threshold, unit, section, quoted text, release, effective date), the citation and verification fields, `requires`, the new inputs' units, cash flow being external, the Earnings windows and comparisons, the single market-maker record, the round-lot flag, the omitted closed-end value, the $100 million pairing, the shape of all three groups (branch names and rule counts), the empty other pathways, the pre-2009 empty set, and the full ruleset count (46 continued, 36 + 23 initial). The wheel holds the four new files. 41 scripted mutations (each new input's unit, a threshold, the market-maker count, a dropped record in each file, the aggregate years, the positive-in-each comparison, each each-of window, a pathway, a branch, a requirement, a cash flow and a market capitalization metric, the equity half, the IPO value, the holder counts and metric, the round-lot quote, the release, the effective date, the series URL, a tier, the listing type, `verified_by`, `retrieved_on`, a revenue and a total assets threshold and window, and three metric or quantity swaps) were each caught, with the files restored byte for byte.
- Options considered: putting the market-maker rule inside the (f)(3) branches (rejected: those branches are not the 5405 standards the rule names); a second input for the 50% clause (rejected, D37); a cash flow concept (deferred until its definition is cited); the closed-end-fund value (omitted); modeling 5315 as three standards (rejected: a standard is "any one of several" and 5315 needs all three groups).
- Why: Global Select is the most demanding tier and the only one built from three independent "meet one" groups, so it is the test of whether the requirement-and-branch mapping carries a real rule without new structure, and it does.
- Revisit if: Nasdaq amends 5315, the cash flow definition is cited (make it a concept), the multi-year series makes the Earnings branch evaluable, or the closed-end-fund value is brought in with its own company type.

### D37: The Nasdaq initial-listing ruleset, first half: Rules 5405 (Global Market) and 5505 (Capital Market), IPO circumstance
- Date: 2026-10-20
- Decision: six rule files under `standards/rulesets/nasdaq/` encode 36 records for initial listing (21 for the Global Market, tier `global`; 15 for the Capital Market), from content the architect supplied and verified against the Nasdaq rulebook (nothing here is recalled; every `quoted_text` is the architect's verbatim wording). Rule 5315 (Global Select) is the second half and gets D38. Every record has `listing_type: initial` and `pathways: [traditional_ipo]`: there is no `listing_circumstance` field by design (Gate 0), so the IPO circumstance is the pathway. The other four pathways return an empty set (`is_empty`, not a pass), which is the Phase 7 gap for seasoned, direct-listing, and affiliated listings.
- Mapping: the same as D36. Tier-common conditions of 5405(a) and 5505(a) carry no `standard_id` and all must hold; each financial standard is a `standard_id` and a company needs any one; "the most recently completed fiscal year or two of the three" is one requirement with a `latest_fiscal_year` branch and a `two_of_three_fiscal_years` branch (5405(b)(1) pre-tax income, 5405(b)(4) total assets and total revenue, 5505(b)(3) net income from continuing operations). Equity, market values, and counts use an `instant` window. Market makers are in each 5405 standard (3, 3, 4, 4) and in 5505(a) (3), as the rules place them. The operating-history conditions reuse `OPERATING_HISTORY` (count of years, threshold 2).
- New inputs: `UNRESTRICTED_PUBLICLY_HELD_SHARES` (shares), `ROUND_LOT_HOLDERS` (count), `NET_TANGIBLE_ASSETS` (usd), and the derived `MARKET_VALUE_OF_UNRESTRICTED_PUBLICLY_HELD_SHARES` (usd; drivers the unrestricted shares and `MARKET_PRICE`). Each is a separately defined term in the rules, so each is its own input, as D36 did for the holders. None can be computed from EDGAR facts, so every rule on them reports them in `requires` and chunk 14 marks it not evaluable.
- Unrestricted market value (MVUPHS): seven records carry only the current value, with no `effective_to`. SR-NASDAQ-2024-084, effective 2025-04-11: 5405(b)(2) $18M, 5405(b)(3) and (b)(4) $20M, 5505(b)(1) and (b)(2) $15M. SR-NASDAQ-2025-068, effective 2025-12-18: 5405(b)(1) $15M and 5505(b)(3) $15M. The prior $8M and $5M versions are an eval-phase backfill, not this commit. Consequence, flagged and not fixed: for an `as_of` before those dates `set_for` returns the standard without its public-float condition, so evaluation must use an `as_of` of 2025-12-18 or later until the backfill. `test_before_the_2025_amendments_a_standard_lacks_its_unrestricted_market_value` pins the behavior for all seven. The 5505(b)(1) quoted text includes the offering-proceeds parenthetical ("this requirement must be satisfied from the offering proceeds"), which is a chunk 14 note: the value is measured on the offering.
- Capital Market bid price, 5505(a)(1): the $4 bid (5505(a)(1)(A)) is the primary branch `bid_4`. The lower-closing-price alternative (5505(a)(1)(B)) is one branch `lower_bid_alternative` that carries the full verbatim text and one rule, `NET_TANGIBLE_ASSETS` in excess of $2,000,000 (`gt`, as the rule says "in excess of"). It is a flagged stand-in, not a model of the rule, by decision: the $3 or $2 closing price and its coupling to the standard met, the $5 million figure for an issuer in operation under three years, and the average revenue of at least $6 million alternative stay in the quoted text. No `mean` aggregation was added and no $18M sum was encoded, because the whole bid condition is price-dependent and not evaluable, so the sub-structure would buy nothing for the margin. Chunk 14 must treat the whole requirement as not evaluable and must never read this branch as satisfied from the net tangible assets figure alone.
- Round lot holders: one rule each (`ROUND_LOT_HOLDERS >= 400` in 5405(a), `>= 300` in 5505(a)). The clause that at least 50% of those holders each hold Unrestricted Securities with a Market Value of at least $2,500 is in the verbatim quoted text and is not modeled as a second input; it is flagged in a comment and for chunk 14.
- Not encoded: Rule 5505(b)(4), the acquisition-company standard. The supplied content gives no figures for it, so encoding it would invent them, and it belongs with the `spac_ipo` pathway in Phase 7.
- Citations (used exactly as supplied): `rule_url` is the 5400-series page for 5405 and the 5500-series page for 5505. Financial standard thresholds and the market-maker, holder, operating-history, and net-tangible-asset conditions: `effective_from` 2009-04-13, SR-NASDAQ-2009-018, https://www.sec.gov/rule-release/34-59663. MVUPHS records: the two amendments above. All records: `verified` true, `retrieved_on` and `verified_on` 2026-10-20, `verified_by` Austin Smith.
- Tests: `tests/test_standards_nasdaq_initial.py` with the hand-written table in `tests/nasdaq_initial_table.py` covers the full 36-row table (tier, standard, requirement, branch, metric, window, comparison, threshold, unit, section, quoted text, release, effective date), the citation and verification fields, `requires`, the new inputs' units and the derived drivers, the round-lot and lower-bid flags, the absence of 5505(b)(4), both tier shapes, the empty other pathways, the pre-2009 empty set, the pre-2025 MVUPHS gap, and that the 46 continued rules are unchanged. The D36 test file is now scoped to `listing_type == continued` (the initial rules share the ruleset), `describe_window` gained an aggregate case for D38, and the old assertion that initial listing is empty was removed because it is no longer true. The wheel holds all six new files. 32 scripted mutations (each new input's unit, the derived drivers, a threshold, a dropped record in three files, a pathway, an MVUPHS date and each release field, the lower-bid comparison, the round-lot quote, a window, a branch, a requirement, the listing type, a tier, the series URL, `verified_by`, `retrieved_on`, a metric, an operating-history threshold, a market-maker count, a standard name, a derived quantity, a concept, an asset threshold) were each caught, with the files restored byte for byte.
- Options considered: a `mean` aggregation or an $18M sum for the average-revenue clause (rejected by the architect: not evaluable, buys nothing); a second input for the 50% round-lot clause (rejected: same); encoding 5505(b)(4) under `spac_ipo` (rejected: no figures supplied); backfilling the prior MVUPHS versions now (deferred to the eval phase).
- Why: initial listing is the larger half of what a company must meet to list, and the ruleset is the cited statement of it, so each condition carries its paragraph, approving release, verbatim text, and verification state, and what cannot be evaluated from EDGAR says so through `requires`.
- Revisit if: the MVUPHS backfill is done, the lower-bid alternative or the 50% round-lot clause needs to be evaluable, Nasdaq amends 5405 or 5505, or the acquisition-company standard is brought in with `spac_ipo`.

### D36: The Nasdaq continued-listing ruleset: Rules 5450 and 5550 as cited records, the stayed floors as dateless records
- Date: 2026-10-13
- Decision: ten rule files under `standards/rulesets/nasdaq/` encode 46 records for continued listing, from content the architect supplied and verified against the Nasdaq rulebook (nothing here is recalled). Rule 5305(e) puts Global Select companies under the Rule 5400 Series, so Global Select and Global Market share Rule 5450 and the Capital Market uses Rule 5550. Each tier needs every tier-common condition plus any one financial standard. D32 calls this entry D33; D33 recorded the renumbering, and it is D36.
- Layout: the Rule 5450 records are written out twice, under `global_select` (ids `nasdaq-5450...-gs-...`) and `global` (`...-g-...`), not as a `set_for` alias, because the schema's record has one tier and an alias would hide a Rule 5305(e) fact in selection code. Four files per 5450 tier (common 3, Equity Standard 4, Market Value Standard 4, Total Assets/Total Revenue Standard 7 records) and two for the Capital Market (common 6, standards 4): 18 + 18 + 10. The loader refuses YAML anchors, so the copies are literal, and a test proves the two are identical in every field but tier, `rule_id`, `standard_id`, and `requirement`.
- Mapping: tier-common conditions carry no `standard_id` and all must hold. Each financial standard is a `standard_id`, and a company needs any one. Where the rule says "the most recently completed fiscal year or two of the three most recently completed fiscal years" (5450(b)(3) total assets and total revenue, 5550(b)(3) net income from continuing operations), one requirement has two branches, `latest_fiscal_year` (window `latest_fiscal_year`) and `two_of_three_fiscal_years` (window `n_of_last_m_fiscal_years`, n 2, m 3), and 5450(b)(3)'s branches each hold total assets and total revenue at $50 million. Every comparison is `gte` and every record is continued listing. All five pathways apply, since continued listing does not depend on how the company entered. The Capital Market Net Income Standard is the worked case: its latest-year branch is evaluable now, because D35 makes income from continuing operations a figure for Trans American and Luckin, and its two-of-three branch needs the multi-year series chunk 12 does not extract, so chunk 14 reports it not evaluable from the window kind alone.
- Metrics: equity is `stockholders_equity` with an `instant` window (the rule states no fiscal year); net income from continuing operations, total assets, and total revenue are the concept targets with fiscal-year windows. Market value of listed securities and of publicly held shares are the derived quantities; publicly held shares, bid price, holders, and market makers are external metrics, so their `requires` is what chunk 14 reports as not evaluable. No condition is dropped: the ruleset test holds the full table of 46 as literals, written by hand and independent of the YAML, and compares every field (tier, standard, requirement, branch, metric, window, threshold, unit, section, quoted text, stayed).
- Holders: `InputKind.HOLDER_COUNT` is split into `TOTAL_HOLDERS` (Rule 5450 "Total Holders") and `PUBLIC_HOLDERS` (Rule 5550 "Public Holders"), both `count`, because they are different measures and the ruleset says which. The one synthetic fixture (`tests/fixtures/standards/synthetic/synthetic-capital-continued.yaml`) and one model test that named `holder_count` were changed to the new names.
- Stayed floors: SR-NASDAQ-2026-004 (filed Jul 22 2026, amended Sep 25 2026) adds a Market Value of Listed Securities floor of $5 million (5450(a)(3), 5550(a)(6)), and the SEC stayed the approval order, so it is not in effect. Each is a tier-common record that is unverified, has no `effective_from`, cites the stayed filing, and notes the stay in its quoted text. The schema change: `effective_from` is nullable, and `None` means never in force. The key stays required (write `~`), so a dropped date cannot silently switch a rule off; `in_force` is false for `None`, so `set_for` and `rule` never return one. One validator enforces both directions of the same rule: a dateless rule is unverified, and a verified rule is dated; a dateless rule also has no `effective_to`. Version checks put an undated version last as a pending successor and skip the overlap test for it, so a dateless version after dated ones is allowed and two dateless versions of one rule fail as a duplicate. The architect's "refuse a None version before a dated one" holds by that ordering, with no separate branch: a branch that sorting makes unreachable would be a guard no mutation can prove, which this project removes.
- Citations (used exactly as supplied): `rule_url` is the 5400-series page for Rule 5450 and the 5500-series page for Rule 5550; the stable records cite SR-NASDAQ-2009-018 at https://www.sec.gov/rule-release/34-59663, `effective_from` 2009-04-13 (the operative date of the 5000-series listing rules), `verified` true, `retrieved_on` and `verified_on` 2026-10-13, `verified_by` Austin Smith; the stayed records cite SR-NASDAQ-2026-004 (the Nasdaq-hosted letter to the Deputy Secretary), no date, unverified. Every `quoted_text` is the architect's verbatim wording. The URLs are series pages, not per-rule anchors, so `rule_section` carries the paragraph.
- For chunk 14: (1) a concept that is an instant balance but tested over a fiscal-year window (total assets in 5450(b)(3)) must be evaluated only on a fiscal-year-end figure, and a quarter-end balance is a gap, because chunk 12's instant selection can return a 10-Q balance. (2) The rule means the same two of three years for total assets and total revenue; the branches encode independent years, which is harmless while that branch is not evaluable, and same-year pairing must be added when the multi-year series makes it evaluable. (3) The bid-price duration semantics (how long the bid must sit below $1) are not encoded; the rule is an instant external `market_price` test, flagged in a comment and not evaluable. (4) A stayed floor, if the stay lifts, becomes a dated version of the same rule.
- Not changed: `operating_history` stays `count` pending initial listing. Initial listing is a separate later commit.
- Tests and mutations: `tests/test_standards_nasdaq_ruleset.py` (with the hand-written table in `tests/nasdaq_table.py`) covers the table, the files, the citations, the stable and stayed states, the stayed floors never being selected, the identical copies, the metric wiring and `requires`, the tier shapes (Global Select and Global: 2 common requirements and 3 standards; Capital: 5 and 3), the two branches, and the policy; `tests/test_standards_undated.py` covers the schema change. The wheel was built and holds all ten rule files and the policy. 29 scripted mutations (the `in_force` result for a dateless rule, either validator direction, a default on `effective_from`, the sort order and skip of dateless versions, the duplicate key, a holder unit and enum member, and 20 edits to the data: a threshold, a dropped record in each ruleset, a window, an edit to only one 5450 copy, a date or verification on a stayed record, quoted text, pathways, a metric, the rulebook series, the release, a branch, a requirement, a window kind, `verified_by`, the effective date, the comparison, and a tier) were each caught, with the files restored byte for byte.
- Options considered: a `set_for` tier alias (rejected: hides a rule fact in code); leaving the stayed floors out until the stay lifts (rejected: the ruleset should say what was proposed and that it is stayed, and a dateless record is never in force); one holder input (rejected: different measures); a default of `None` for `effective_from` (rejected: a forgotten date would silently disable a rule).
- Why: the ruleset is the cited, versioned statement of what Nasdaq requires, so every threshold carries its rule paragraph, approving release, verbatim text, and verification state, and a rule that is proposed but stayed is recorded without ever being applied.
- Revisit if: the stay on SR-NASDAQ-2026-004 lifts, Nasdaq amends 5450 or 5550, the chunk 14 evaluation needs same-year pairing sooner, or the series pages stop being stable enough to cite without an anchor.

### D35: Income from continuing operations falls back to net income only when pre-tax minus tax corroborates it
- Date: 2026-10-13
- Decision: when an issuer tags neither `IncomeLossFromContinuingOperations` element (D33), net income stands in for `INCOME_FROM_CONTINUING_OPERATIONS` at an accession and full-year period (same accession, start, and end, USD) only if three facts at that key reconcile exactly: pre-tax income (the `PRETAX_INCOME` candidates in alias order, the first with a fact at the key), `IncomeTaxExpenseBenefit`, and net income, with pre-tax minus tax equal to net income. The subtraction runs in a 100-digit decimal context (`exact_difference`), because the default 28 digits would round 10**30 + 1 down to 10**30 and report an identity that does not hold. A missing fact, a fact at another accession, period, or unit, or two different values for one tag at one key means the key is not a candidate; nothing is guessed. The target stays the parent measure, consistent with parent-first D33.
- Which net income: `NetIncomeLoss` (the parent measure) needs no witness. `ProfitLoss` is the total including the noncontrolling interest, so it counts only with a witness: a nonzero `NetIncomeLossAttributableToNoncontrollingInterest` fact at the key vetoes it; a present and zero NCI income fact passes it (`nci_income_is_zero`); with no NCI fact, `NetIncomeLossAvailableToCommonStockholdersBasic` equal to `ProfitLoss` passes it (`common_equals_total`, since NCI income and preferred deductions are then both nil); anything else refuses, because absent NCI facts prove nothing. The common-stockholder figure is a witness only and is never a value. If both identities hold, `NetIncomeLoss` wins. If neither does, or the veto or missing witness blocks `ProfitLoss`, the result is `concept_not_found`, never the total. Discontinued operations break the identity the same way and leave a gap (no fixture has them, so that case is synthetic).
- Selection: corroborated candidates are added after the explicit tags in D31's freshest-period-then-order rule, so at the same period an explicit tag wins the tie, a fresher corroborated period beats a stale explicit tag, and the corroborated candidate is kept out of the cross-tag diagnostic. The citation records the tag actually used.
- Evidence: `FinancialValue.corroborated_by: IdentityEvidence | None = None` (frozen, strict, `extra="forbid"`: `pretax_tag`, `pretax_value`, `tax_tag`, `tax_value`, `witness`), re-verified by the model: pre-tax minus tax must equal the figure exactly, and evidence is allowed only on this target, so forged evidence is rejected. Reported-currency twins (D34) still follow the tag used.
- Luckin NCI resolution (raw JSON): Luckin tags NCI income only through FY2021 (USD -298,000, -2,128,000, +17,000; CNY -2,074,000, -13,885,000, +108,000) and nothing from FY2022; the NCI balance (`MinorityInterest`) is tagged for 2019 and 2020 only. It tags no `NetIncomeLoss`, only `ProfitLoss` and the common-stockholder figure, which are equal for FY2022 onward and unequal before (FY2020: -5,602,990,000 less NCI income -13,885,000 is -5,589,105,000, exactly the common-stockholder figure). Pre-tax minus tax equals `ProfitLoss` in every Luckin year FY2019 to FY2025, NCI or not, so the identity alone cannot tell the parent figure from the total; that is why the total needs a witness. So `ProfitLoss` is Luckin's parent figure only from FY2022. Outcome: FY2025 (accession 0001104659-26-035712) is evaluable from `ProfitLoss` = 513,877,000 (753,632,000 - 239,755,000), witness `common_equals_total`, CNY twin 3,600,382,000; FY2022 is evaluable (70,790,000); bounded to FY2021 or earlier it is a gap, which is the real-data proof that the total is refused when NCI income is nonzero.
- Which net income tag each issuer lands on: Trans American `NetIncomeLoss` (FY2024, accession 0001683168-25-005173, -2,808,894 - 0, witness `parent_tag`; it tags no `ProfitLoss`, NCI, or minority fact); Luckin `ProfitLoss` (FY2022 onward); GE (trimmed) none, a gap, because it has no pre-tax, tax, `NetIncomeLoss`, or `ProfitLoss` fact. Trans American's FY2022 has two accessions with pre-tax -895,880 and -895,881 against tax 26,937 and net -922,817, and only the first reconciles; its FY2020 has a pre-tax fact but no tax or net income fact, so nothing is produced.
- Known limitation, recorded and deliberately not fixed: a company with no noncontrolling interest but with preferred dividends, that does not tag a zero NCI income fact, is a gap on the `ProfitLoss` path, because the common-stockholder figure then cannot equal the total and absent NCI facts prove nothing. That errs toward a missed figure rather than a wrong one. The same company is fine if it tags `NetIncomeLoss`.
- Tests and mutations: synthetic documents cover every branch (parent tag, each witness alone, each refusal, discontinued operations, each missing or mismatched fact, precedence, restated accessions, exactness at 31 digits); `tests/test_extraction_corroboration_fixtures.py` holds the raw-JSON constants for Luckin FY2019 to FY2021 (the refusal) and FY2025, Trans American, and GE; two headline rows (Trans American `NetIncomeLoss`, Luckin `ProfitLoss`) feed the existing oracle tests. The old gap test now covers GE only, and the Trans American snapshot is nine figures with no gaps, Luckin seven and two. 36 scripted mutations (including removing the witness, which would return the total as the parent, and removing the NCI veto) were each caught, with the files restored byte for byte.
- Options considered: only when no explicit candidate has any usable fact (rejected: it would keep a stale explicit tag over a fresher corroborated period); `ProfitLoss` without a witness (rejected: the identity holds in Luckin's NCI years, so it returns the total as the parent); using the common-stockholder figure as a value (rejected: it is after preferred dividends, a different measure); accepting a near match to a tolerance (rejected: restated accessions differ by one unit in real data and only the exact one is the same statement).
- Why: the Net Income Standard must test the parent's after-tax continuing-operations figure, and an issuer with no discontinued operations and no NCI states it implicitly; admitting that case only when the arithmetic proves it keeps the cited figure honest and makes the common case evaluable.
- Revisit if: a real filer tags preferred dividends without a zero NCI fact often enough to matter (add a preferred-dividends witness), the Nasdaq text means the total rather than the parent figure, or a filer tags discontinued operations in a way the identity should handle rather than refuse.

### D34: A translated figure carries the reported-currency twin and the implied rate; D31's flag enriched
- Date: 2026-10-13
- Decision: when a USD figure is a convenience translation (D31), `FinancialValue` now carries the figure the filer actually reported, not just a bool. `reported_twins: tuple[ReportedTwin, ...] = ()`, where `ReportedTwin` (frozen, strict, `extra="forbid"`) holds `unit` (for example `CNY`), `value` (exactly as reported, nothing converted), `period` (identical to the figure's), and `implied_rate` (the reported value divided by the USD value, that is units of the reported currency per one USD; `None` when the USD value is zero). A reader, and chunk 14, can therefore show the home-currency figure beside the translated USD one and say at what rate. `is_convenience_translation` stays a stored field. A computed property would be tidier, but `computed_field` over `@property` needs a `type: ignore` under this mypy plugin, which the project bans, so a validator keeps the flag equal to `bool(reported_twins)` in both directions, exactly as `unit` and `period` already track the citation. The tuple is the right type for a cross-border tool even though no fixture has a second twin unit.
- Validation (`FinancialValue` keeps its unit and period checks): the flag equals `bool(reported_twins)`; twin units are unique; no twin is in the figure's own unit; each twin's period equals the figure's; and `implied_rate` equals the recomputed `translation_rate(twin.value, figure.value)`, so a forged or stale rate is rejected. `ReportedTwin` itself refuses a rate that is not positive. The formula lives in one function, `translation_rate`, used both to build a twin and to check one. A pair that cannot be a translation of each other (opposite signs, or a zero reported value against a nonzero USD value) fails these checks as `InputValidationError`, which `build_figure` already reports as `MalformedPayload` at `...units.USD.reported_twins`, so no raw error escapes.
- Selection: `_has_twin` (a bool) became `_reported_twins`, returning one `(unit, fact)` per other unit that holds a fact with the same accession, start, and end, sorted by unit, taking the latest-filed fact if a unit repeats the period. Money only, as before: a share count never has a twin. `Selection` keeps `is_convenience_translation` as a property over the tuple, so the existing callers and tests are unchanged and all 1033 of them passed before any new test was written.
- Evidence (Luckin, read from the raw JSON): 865 of its 877 USD facts have a CNY twin; none has a second twin unit, none has a twin unit holding more than one distinct value, none has opposite signs, and 3 USD values are zero (so a rate of `None` is a real case). The implied rate differs a little by line because the filer's USD figures are rounded: Assets 7.00630048521297, equity 7.006302430572371, net income 7.0063108487050405, revenue 7.006300085645343, all within 0.0001 of the 7.0063 the filer states in `ForeignCurrencyExchangeRateTranslation1` (unit label `USD/CNY`, value CNY per USD, dated 2025-12-26 although the period ends 2025-12-31). So the rate is a derived approximation, held at full precision and never rounded in storage; `test_the_implied_rate_is_within_a_ten_thousandth_of_the_stated_rate` pins the relationship. Citing the filer's stated rate as its own fact is a possible later improvement and is not done here. Nothing is converted anywhere.
- Oracle: `tests/test_extraction_twins_fixtures.py` holds the USD and CNY constants for Luckin's Assets, equity, net income, and revenue as literals; `test_each_pair_of_constants_is_what_the_raw_fixture_json_says` checks each against the raw JSON with no extraction code, the stated 7.0063 is checked the same way, and the extraction tests then compare the twin's value to the CNY constant and its rate to the CNY constant divided by the USD constant. Every Luckin figure has exactly one CNY twin, and no Trans American or GE figure has any.
- Mutation proofs: 26 scripted mutations (invert, drop the zero guard of, or round the rate; allow a zero rate or skip the sign check; remove the flag/twins check or keep only one direction of it; allow duplicate units, a twin in the figure's own unit, a differing period, or an unrecomputed rate; do not sort twin units, take the first instead of the latest-filed fact, drop the accession, start, or end match, count the figure's own unit, or allow twins on share counts; force the flag true or false; convert the twin value, invert the rate, drop the twins, force the flag false, or give the twin an instant period when building the figure) were each caught, with the files restored byte for byte.
- Options considered: a single optional twin (rejected: a cross-border tool should not silently pick one when a filer reports a figure in more than one unit); a derived `is_convenience_translation` (rejected: the `type: ignore` the project bans); storing the rate rounded (rejected: it would hide that each line implies a slightly different rate); citing the filer's stated rate now (deferred: it is a separate fact with its own date, and the derived rate needs no extra lookup); making the flag and twins one field (rejected: chunk 14 and the existing tests read the bool).
- Why: a USD figure that is only a point-in-time translation of a CNY number should say so, show the number, and show the rate, because a threshold crossing can be an artifact of the translation rate and a reader has to be able to see that.
- Revisit if: a filer reports a figure in two other currencies and the ordering by unit proves unhelpful, the chunk 14 display wants the filer's stated rate instead of the implied one, or a real pair fails the positive-rate check and turns out to be a legitimate (for example sign-flipping) presentation.

### D33: Income from continuing operations is the ninth extraction target; D31's "eight" amended
- Date: 2026-10-13
- Decision: `TargetConcept.INCOME_FROM_CONTINUING_OPERATIONS` is added, appended last so every earlier ordering (snapshot figures, gap lists, test expectations) is unchanged. It is a flow concept in USD. Chunk 13's research found that the Nasdaq Net Income Standard tests net income from continuing operations after tax, which is neither `NET_INCOME` (total net income) nor `PRETAX_INCOME`. D31 says eight targets and is left as written; this entry amends it. Wiring: the enum member, an `ALIASES` entry, and a `CONCEPT_UNITS` entry (USD), so `test_every_registry_covers_every_member` and `test_concept_units_agree_with_the_unit_extraction_reads` keep passing.
- Tags, confirmed by the architect against the FASB and XBRL US taxonomy (not observed in any fixture): first `us-gaap:IncomeLossFromContinuingOperations`, which the taxonomy defines as the after-tax figure attributable to the parent (after deducting the noncontrolling-interest portion), then `us-gaap:IncomeLossFromContinuingOperationsIncludingPortionAttributableToNoncontrollingInterest`, the total. There is no separate `AttributableToReportingEntity` element. Parent first, which keeps this target consistent with net income and equity (XBRL US guidance prefers the parent-attributable element); the cross-tag diagnostic surfaces the total when the noncontrolling interest is nonzero. This overrides my planning recommendation of the total first, which assumed the bare tag was the pre-NCI subtotal; the taxonomy says it is not. Still a flag for chunk 13: confirm that the Net Income Standard means the parent's figure.
- Name traps, excluded and tested absent per target (the earlier global trap test could not hold, because `PRETAX_INCOME` legitimately carries `BeforeIncomeTaxes`): every before-income-taxes tag including the `Domestic` and `Foreign` subsets (that is `PRETAX_INCOME`); `EffectiveIncomeTaxRateContinuingOperations` (a rate in unit `pure`, observed in Luckin at 0.32, which is why matching on "ContinuingOperations" is unsafe); the per-basic-share, per-diluted-share, and per-share continuing-operations tags (USD/shares); every discontinued-operations tag (the opposite figure); and `NetIncomeLoss` and `ProfitLoss` (total net income, a different target). A new test also pins that no tag feeds two targets.
- Survey of the three recorded fixtures (read from the raw JSON): none tags either element, and none carries a discontinued-operations income tag (GE has only `AssetsOfDisposalGroupIncludingDiscontinuedOperationCurrent`, an asset). So until the corroborated fallback of D35, the target is a `concept_not_found` gap for Trans American, Luckin, and GE, which `test_income_from_continuing_operations_is_a_gap_for_every_recorded_issuer` pins. The fixtures do carry the identity that makes the fallback possible, as raw constants in `IDENTITY_ROWS`: pre-tax minus income-tax expense equals net income exactly, for Luckin in USD (753,632,000 - 239,755,000 = 513,877,000, which is `ProfitLoss`), for Luckin in CNY (5,280,175,000 - 1,679,793,000 = 3,600,382,000), and for Trans American (-2,808,894 - 0 = `NetIncomeLoss`).
- Tests: the alias table pins the order, period kind, and unit; synthetic documents (the tag is unobserved, as `SalesRevenueNet` is) cover parent-first with a surfaced total, equal values raising no diagnostic, a fresher total winning with the switch visible in the citation, a total-only filer, each look-alike alone giving `ConceptNotFound` (eight cases), a pre-tax tag feeding only `PRETAX_INCOME`, quarterly-only and instant-only giving `PeriodUnavailable`, a CNY-only filer giving `UnitUnavailable`, null fiscal labels, and the money-only twin flag. Existing snapshot tests now expect the new gap (Trans American one, Luckin three, GE six). 16 scripted mutations (swap the order, add each look-alike or a shared tag to the list, change the period or unit kind, drop or alter the unit entry, move the member, rename its value) were each caught, with the files restored byte for byte.
- Options considered: the total first (rejected: the taxonomy defines the bare tag as the parent figure); placing the member beside the other income targets (rejected: reorders every snapshot and explicit list); folding total net income in as an alias now (rejected: it is a different measure and silently wrong whenever discontinued operations exist; D35 admits it only when the identity corroborates it).
- Numbering: D32 calls the Nasdaq ruleset entry D33. With this follow-up the numbers are D33 (this target), D34 (the reported-currency twin), D35 (the corroborated net income fallback), and the ruleset entry becomes D36. D32 is left as written.
- Why: the Net Income Standard must test the figure it names, and a figure that merely resembles it by name or sits beside it in the statements would turn a margin result into a wrong number that looks cited.
- Revisit if: the Nasdaq text means the total rather than the parent figure (swap the two tags), or a real filer tags a variant that neither element covers.

### D32: Listing standards as a versioned ruleset: typed schema, strict YAML loader, rulesets packaged in the library
- Date: 2026-10-12
- Decision: `standards/` holds the schema and loader for the Nasdaq listing standards (D7, D10, P2-H). This commit encodes no Nasdaq content: every threshold, quotation, and condition is Gate 1 content for the next commit (D33), and the tests run on synthetic records marked "not Nasdaq content" (`tests/fixtures/standards/synthetic/`, ids `SYN-`, urls `example.invalid`; a test enforces the marker). Layout: `enums.py`, `quantities.py` (inputs, derived quantities, units), `metrics.py` (metrics and windows), `rules.py` (the rule record, citation, verification, policy), `ruleset.py` (grouping and as-of selection), `checks.py` (cross-record checks), `loader.py`, `yaml_loader.py` (the only module that imports PyYAML; a test pins that). `load_ruleset(source)` reads every `*.yaml` rule file and the required `policy.yaml` from a directory-like source and returns a `Ruleset`.
- Location, a refinement of D7: the ruleset YAML lives inside the package, `src/listinglens/standards/rulesets/nasdaq/`, and is found with `importlib.resources`, not in a root `standards/` directory. Reason: root-level data is not packaged in the Hatch wheel, so a deployed server could not load its own rules. Verified rather than assumed: `uv build --wheel` contains `listinglens/standards/rulesets/nasdaq/policy.yaml`, and with the wheel itself on `PYTHONPATH` (so the package imports from inside the zip) `default_source().iterdir()` lists it and `load_ruleset()` loads it. `test_a_ruleset_loads_from_inside_a_zip_archive_as_it_does_from_a_wheel` keeps the zip path covered in the suite; the loader takes any directory-like `Traversable`, so tests pass a `tmp_path`.
- The record, one requirement per record (D7): `rule_id` (stable across versions), `exchange`, `tier` (`global_select`, `global`, `capital`), `listing_type` (`initial`, `continued`), `pathways` (non-empty; every rule is `traditional_ipo` for now), `standard_id` and `standard_name` (set together, or both omitted for a tier-common rule), `requirement` and `branch`, `metric`, `window`, `comparison` (`gte`, `gt`, `lte`, `lt`), `threshold` (exact `Decimal`), `threshold_unit` (`usd`, `usd_per_share`, `shares`, `count`), `effective_from` (inclusive), `effective_to` (exclusive, or open), `citation` (rule section, https rule url, verbatim quotation, retrieval date, optional SEC release and url, which come as a pair), `verification` (a verified rule records who and when, and not before the citation was retrieved), optional `proxy` and `translation_policy`. Initial rules are scoped to the IPO circumstance by `pathways` (Gate 0 S2); there is no separate listing-circumstance field, and the other circumstances are Phase 7.
- Grouping (Gate 0 S8): a Standard is the rules sharing a `standard_id`, and all of its requirements must hold. A StandardSet is every tier-common requirement plus at least one standard. Gate 0 S4 found compound shapes a flat record cannot hold, so alternatives exist below the standard level too: rules sharing a `requirement` are alternatives by `branch` (a requirement holds if any one branch holds, and a branch holds if all of its rules hold). That expresses "the most recent fiscal year, or two of the last three" (two branches of one requirement) and "one value, or a pair with equity" (a one-rule branch against a two-rule branch). A rule with no `requirement` is its own requirement. Selection is `Ruleset.set_for(exchange, tier, listing_type, pathway, as_of)`; an empty result is `is_empty`, never a pass.
- Metrics and inputs (Gate 0 S6): a tagged union of `concept` (a chunk 12 `TargetConcept`), `derived` (a registered `DerivedQuantity` with declared inputs), and `external` (an `InputKind`: `market_price`, `publicly_held_shares`, `holder_count`, `market_maker_count`, `operating_history`). `StandardRule.requires` is computed from the metric, never set on the record, so there is one source of truth: a rule that needs `market_price` is not evaluable from EDGAR alone, and chunk 14 reports it as such instead of failing it. Two derived quantities are registered because Gate 0 names them, with only their non-EDGAR drivers declared; Gate 1 completes each definition from the cited text. The threshold unit must equal the metric's unit, checked by the model. Open flag for Gate 1: `operating_history` is a count of fiscal years, so it uses unit `count`, because Gate 0 S7 gave no years unit; confirm against the rule text.
- Windows (Gate 0 S5): `instant`, `latest_fiscal_year`, `n_of_last_m_fiscal_years` (needs 1 <= n <= m), and `aggregate_over_fiscal_years` (years >= 2, sum). Every multi-year form tests the rule's own comparison and threshold, so there is no separate per-year-floor field: "positive in all three years" is `n=3, m=3`, `gt`, `0`; "a minimum in each of the two most recent" is `n=2, m=2`; the three-year aggregate is its own rule. The two multi-year forms (`n_of_last_m_fiscal_years` and `aggregate_over_fiscal_years`) are exactly what chunk 12 cannot feed (it extracts only the latest period), so those rules are `not_evaluable` until a series extension exists; the schema carries them either way. A check that a flow concept uses a fiscal-year window is left to commit 2's consistency test, which can read `extraction.aliases` without making `standards/` depend on `extraction/`.
- History (Gate 0 S9, S10): thresholds are moving by date, so every record carries `effective_from` and `effective_to`, versions of one `rule_id` must not overlap and must keep their tier, listing type, and standard, and `effective_from` is inclusive while `effective_to` is exclusive so versions tile without a gap. Chunk 13 ships current rules only; history is backfilled into the same schema when the eval needs it. Effective status of recent amendments is confirmed at Gate 1.
- The strict loader neutralizes the YAML 1.1 behaviors that would change a value silently, each pinned by a test and a mutation: duplicate keys are rejected (PyYAML keeps the last), non-string keys are rejected, anchors, aliases, and explicit tags are rejected before any value is built, `yes`, `no`, `on`, `off`, and capitalized `True` stay strings (only `true` and `false` are booleans), integers are plain decimals only (`0755`, `0x1F`, `1_000`, `1:30`, and `1e3` stay strings), a decimal token becomes an exact `Decimal` from its source text (an 18 digit threshold keeps every digit, D27), dates stay strings so the shared ISO gate decides them (D28), the file is capped at 1 MiB, and only one document is allowed. A key with no value is the empty string, not null, on purpose: a half-edited `effective_to:` must not silently become an open-ended rule, and every field rejects an empty string; `~` or `null` is the explicit way to say none. A merge key `<<` is only an ordinary key that the schema then refuses. Every failure is a `StandardsConfigError(path, record, field, detail)` with a bounded detail that never echoes input; no `ValidationError`, `YAMLError`, `OSError`, or `KeyError` crosses the boundary. PyYAML 6.0.3 and `types-pyyaml` are pinned exactly; `uv audit` found no vulnerabilities in the 28 locked packages.
- Config models are not model-level strict, unlike the extraction models (D26): YAML supplies strings for enum members, which strict mode rejects. Each int, bool, number, and date field carries its own strict gate instead (a float, string, or bool threshold is refused; `verified` must be a real boolean; a datetime or a numeric string is not a date). A typed caller may pass a `date` object.
- Two shared pieces moved to `core/` so `standards/` does not import `ingestion/`. By the architect's ruling on shared helpers, `core/validation.py` now holds the bounded error summary that `parse_or_raise` used (count, first three locations, 2,000 characters, input never echoed), and `MalformedPayload` and `StandardsConfigError` both use it; the 788 existing tests passed untouched, which is the proof of no drift. Not covered by a ruling and recorded for review: the ISO date gate moved from `ingestion/payloads/dates.py` to `core/dates.py` (`git mv`, three import lines and the test file renamed to `test_core_dates.py`); D28 is left as written and still names the old path. It was the one piece of the standards schema that would otherwise have imported from `ingestion`, or been copied, and D28 rejected a copy per module.
- Policy (rulings 6, 7, 10), recorded in `rulesets/nasdaq/policy.yaml` with a stated basis: `translation: allow_flagged` (a convenience-translated USD figure may feed a USD threshold unconverted and the result carries the flag; a cited rule on Q9 may override it per rule) and `proxy: off` (a rule's explicit proxy is used only when this is on, and the result says "via proxy"). These are our decisions, not Nasdaq claims, so they carry no verification. A rule with `verified: false` may be evaluated by chunk 14 and is marked unverified in the result; the Phase 5 eval and release gate refuse to certify on unverified rules.
- Dropped as unprovable by mutation, per the project rule: `utf-8-sig` decoding (PyYAML already skips a leading byte order mark; the BOM test stays as a pin), an explicit non-finite threshold check (pydantic's `Decimal` already refuses NaN and infinity, as D27 found; a test pins it), and a "no empty standard" check (a standard is derived from its rules, so it cannot be empty).
- Evidence: 58 scripted mutations (each YAML guard, the record gates, every cross-record check, the metrics and windows, selection and grouping, the loader's file rules, and the shared summary) were run with every file restored byte for byte. Of the four first uncaught, two guards were the dead code above, one mutation was ineffective (it widened a regex but not the resolver's first-character list) and was redone, and one needed a test that the file order does not depend on the source's listing order, which a reversed-listing source now proves. Every remaining mutation is caught.
- Options considered: `tomllib` (rejected by the architect: the signed decision is YAML, and the strict loader neutralizes its footguns); a root `standards/` directory (rejected: not packaged in the wheel); a `listing_circumstance` field now (rejected: only the IPO circumstance exists until Phase 7, and `pathways` scopes it); a separate per-year-floor field on the window (rejected: it would duplicate the rule's own threshold); model-level strict config models (rejected: strict rejects the strings YAML supplies); copying the date gate into `standards/` (rejected: D28).
- Why: the ruleset is the thing the margin engine grades against, so its shape must make a wrong rule unrepresentable (a unit that does not match its metric, an unverified rule that claims a date, overlapping versions, a number that went through a float), its source must be auditable to a quotation and a release, and it must load the same from a source tree and an installed wheel.
- Revisit if: Gate 1 shows a condition the record cannot express (for example a conditional threshold), the rule text gives `operating_history` a unit other than a count of years, a standard is renamed between versions (the name check is per standard, across all versions), or the packaged data grows enough to need a different resource layout.

### D31: Cited financial figures from companyfacts; aliasing in code, freshest period then alias order
- Date: 2026-10-12
- Decision: `extraction/` turns a `CompanyFactsDocument` into eight `FinancialValue`s, one per `TargetConcept` (stockholders' equity, net income, pre-tax income, total assets, revenue, cash, shares outstanding, public float), each carrying an `XbrlLocator`. `ExtractionService(CompanyFactsRepository)` has two calls: `extract_snapshot(company)` is the primary one (one fetch, every target, a `ConceptGap` with a reason for each target that cannot be extracted) and `extract_figure(company, concept)` raises the typed error instead. Layout: `domain/financials.py` (types), `extraction/aliases.py` (the map), `periods.py` (eligibility), `selection.py` (the algorithm), `figures.py` (selection to cited figure; named `figures.py` rather than the planned `locators.py` because it also builds the diagnostics), `service.py`. `extraction/` imports no `httpx`, transport, cache, or client module; `test_extraction_layer_does_no_http_and_imports_no_transport` enforces it with `ast`, so only real imports count.
- Aliasing (P2-F, signed off in planning and recorded here because it was never written down): concept aliasing is an in-code frozen priority map, not YAML; the tag actually chosen is recorded in the citation (`citation.concept` and `citation.taxonomy`); and disagreement between tags or between accessions is surfaced as a diagnostic, never resolved silently. It stays in code because it is parsing logic keyed to XBRL tag names that changes only together with a test, whereas listing standards (D7) are policy data that needs an effective date and a primary-source citation per rule. YAML is deferred unless Phase 7 foreign-private-issuer variety forces it. The map is a `MappingProxyType`, pinned entry by entry (order included) in `test_extraction_aliases.py`. Name traps are excluded on purpose and tested absent: `LiabilitiesAndStockholdersEquity` (liabilities plus equity), `NetIncomeLossAvailableToCommonStockholdersBasic` (after preferred dividends), the `IncomeLossFromContinuingOperationsBeforeIncomeTaxes` `Domestic` and `Foreign` subsets (Luckin tags `Domestic`), and the `WeightedAverage` share counts (not point-in-time).
- Refinement of P2-F's "first-present-wins": the candidate whose latest period is freshest wins, and alias order breaks ties. Literal first-present-wins would return a stale year for an issuer that abandoned a tag (for example `Revenues` for `RevenueFromContractWithCustomerExcludingAssessedTax`); the synthetic stale-tag test pins that, and on all three recorded fixtures both rules choose the same tag. The rule has a cost that is now explicit: candidates can be different measures, so freshness can switch the measure of a figure (parent-only `StockholdersEquity` staler, `StockholdersEquityIncludingPortionAttributableToNoncontrollingInterest` fresher, the fresher one wins). That is pinned by `test_a_fresher_alternate_measure_wins_and_the_citation_records_the_switch`, in both directions, and it is never silent because the chosen tag is in the citation.
